The message names the chain and both groupings, `-(1.abs)` and `(-1).abs`, with the value of each when every suffix is a `.word` naming an L1 prelude word (`interp::prim`) that the source never writes bare, so no binder can shadow it; the values are computed by `zed_kernel::eval` on `Term::Prim` chains. Notes at the literal, the chain and the whole expression, then the two repairs. Accepted: `-(1).abs`, `-(1.abs)`, `(-1).abs`, `-x.abs`, `x - 1.abs`, `!1.abs`, and `-1.abs ** 2` (the operand is a power, as `-x ** 2` is `-(x ** 2)`). Spec branch wt/osp-neglit-spec: one sentence in sec:grammar's disambiguation paragraph; PEG unchanged; `check` and `fences` give the same result as main, `cases` the same 2 wrong `(T,)` cases as main. Tests: `l01_29_diagnostics/neglit.rs` (the full rendered message, the row and the marked unit `zed/offsets.zed`, both repairs run and print 1 and -1, 19 corner cases, a float, a unit function and a shadowed `abs` with no values) and two bracketing cases in `zed/grammar/cases.txt`; the corpus counts of l01_33, l01_34 and l01_36 rise by the new marked line. Scaling (COMPLEXITY.md F60): n negated chains at 400 and 3,200, 8.0 times; one chain of n words, 7.8 times (limit 24). Negative control, run once and not committed: listing the bare names again at each rejection fails the first at 59.7 times. |
| SCALE-bytes | done | `zed_tests::scaling` checks bytes allocated as a second measure next to instructions: a run of size 8n may allocate at most 24 times the bytes of size n, as for instructions. The bytes come from `zed-alloc-count`, a global allocator in the test binaries that adds the size of each `alloc`, `alloc_zeroed` and `realloc` (new size) to a thread-local count and forwards to `System`; freeing counts nothing. The count is per thread, so tests running beside a measured run do not change it (test `the_bytes_of_a_run_do_not_depend_on_tests_running_beside_it`: the same count on 20 runs while 4 threads allocate); a run that starts a thread uses `scaling::on_a_thread`, which adds the thread's bytes to the caller's. Negative control (worktree on 89ccb6c^, the Vec-copying `resolve`, not committed): bytes 12,179,753 against 546,333,984 (44.9 times), so the check fails; the instructions-only check passes. Every scaling test in the local suite was run with the new measure: two in `l01_33_lowering` fail on bytes only (40.3 and 32.6 times), from `zir::validate` formatting its path strings at every node (COMPLEXITY.md Z17, item ZIR-validate-bytes); T2-mid already fixes it (checked in a worktree at b35b33f: all 12 pass), so the two check instructions only, with a comment, until T2-mid merges |
| R10-stack | done | COMPLEXITY.md R10. Native code no longer overflows the stack of the thread that runs it: `zed_rt::Stack` (new, `crates/zed-rt/src/stack.rs`) maps a stack as large as memory and swap together with `MAP_NORESERVE` and a guard page below it, and `zed_native::run_at` runs `zed_root` on it through `psm::on_stack`, so recursion depth and frame size are limited by memory as the heap is, with no depth limit and no size switch; generated code is unchanged. `zed-rt` now allows `unsafe` (deny, with `#[expect]` at each block), as `zed-native` does, for the mapping and the switch only; new dependencies `libc` and `psm`. Tests: `l01_36_cranelift::deep_calls` (2): `linked_list` of 10,000 and 80,000 calls on a 2 MiB thread gives the kernel's value, and scales 6.12 times from 10,000 to 80,000 (limit 24); `deep_heap::a_function_of_150000_concatenations_runs_on_the_stack_of_a_test_thread` replaces `a_frame_larger_than_the_stack_stops_at_the_guard_page`, which recorded the overflow; 3 unit tests in `stack.rs`. Controls, run once and not committed: main aborts with a stack overflow on the 10,000-call test and on the scaling test; an O(n²) spin in `rt_callable` fails scaling at 35.0 times. The wide function is not run at 8 times: native compilation in the test profile takes about 0.2 ms per node, so 150,000 nodes take about 20 s; `tools/run_tests.sh` gives each `deep_heap` test its own piece. Design: /home/luke/Work/wt/r10-stack-design.md |

Complexity tradeoff at `Rat::literal_text`: the part that is not a power of 10 is printed in full (a literal's own digits; a computed constant's digits), and a computed quotient that is not a decimal prints as `1r/3r`, which the type syntax cannot write. Tests in `l01_25_preimage_catalog::s22`: `remainders_over_many_periods_scale`, `remainders_over_many_periods_keep_their_preimages`, `remainder_splits_at_their_corners_keep_their_preimages`. Open: terms the summary still does not read, and the merged middle periods over many periods (recorded at `split_residues`). |
| T6-bound-load | done | Three flaws left by T6 (9a413d5). (1) The `L .. U -> V` bound check ran in elaboration only, so a parameter's annotation was never checked on some paths. Now `Tables` lists every written type once (parameters, lambda parameters, defaults, tests, `is`), and `check::check_with`, which every path calls, checks their bounds in one pass (`Ck::bounds`, `elab::kernel_type`). Load, check and batch give the same message (`l01_23_subtyping::bound_paths`, `l01_29_diagnostics::batch`). Elaboration no longer stops at a failed bound, so `x is (#a .. #b -> int)` now also reports that `is` cannot test a function type. (2) A rejection whose check the kernel refused now names the cause with `Ty::refused`: the overload message "does not take", in both its forms (no arrow meets the argument, `refused_among`; no arrow is left), the literal failure and the need-result check (`check_on`). A decided failure is unchanged: an arrow that some argument fails by a decided check gives no cause (`refusals::an_overload_rejected_by_a_decided_check_names_no_cause`). The kernel's value builder (`types/empty.rs` `find_rep`) built no value for a cofinite set of rationals or floats, so `f64 <: int` and `f64 <: rat` were `Refused(NoWitness)`, and `x + f64(1)` with `x = 1r` would have reported that cause; each carrier now gives one of its first values not listed (`refusals::a_cofinite_set_of_rationals_or_floats_has_a_value`). Both tests fail on 1c40ccc. Refused inputs cannot be reached from source: the checker's `Ty` drops a written difference of tuples (`((triple) \ (boxes),)` is shown as `((triple),)`), and operator and prelude arrows take carriers. So the refused cases are tested through `check::operator` with kernel types: the seven-box cover (`TupleSplit`) and a morphism (`NoWitness`) on `neg`, `+`, `<` and `!` (`refusals::an_overload_whose_check_was_refused_says_why`). The decided cases are tested end to end. A literal against a tuple or morphism part is always decided, because a scalar never meets one. The default check with a class goes through `flow`, which uses carrier masks and so is always decided. (3) An untracked declared type prints through the kernel printer (`check::declared_text`), and the text parses back as the same type (`l01_29_diagnostics::untracked`). No source program reaches that message: `denoted` gives the unknown only for an empty `&`, which the grammar does not produce, and `_` in a type is layer L10. Scaling: 250 and 2,000 bounded parameters through `load`, counted in instructions by `scaling::check_order` (`bound_paths::the_bound_walk_scales_linearly`, within the limit of 24 times). The negative control, a quadratic walk that was not committed, did 60.2 times the instructions (2.20e9 to 1.32e11) and fails it. Not done (follow-up T6-ports): `Tables::written` leaves out output-port types, so `f() -> (out: #a .. #b -> int) { 0 }` with `main() = 0;` is accepted; more broadly the port types of a function other than `main` are not checked at all (`f() -> (out: int) { 0 }` is accepted; only main's ports are checked, `run.rs`). |
| T6-ports | done | Two holes left by T6-bound-load. (1) `Tables::written` left out output-port types, so the bound of a port type was never checked: `main() -> (out: #a .. #b -> int)` gave the input-port message of `run::program`, never the bound error; each port's type now joins `written` in the order of the text (`elab::Tables::collect_stmt`), and load, check and batch report the bound (`l01_23_subtyping::bound_paths::a_port_whose_morphism_bound_fails_is_rejected`). (2) The ports of a function other than `main` were not checked at all: `f() -> (out: #a .. #b -> int) { 0 }` with `main() = 0;` and `log(positional s) -> (out: Files)` were accepted. `resolve` now rejects such a port, at the port, with "output port `out` of `f` is `Lines` at layer L1" (DESIGN.md step L1.19: at L1 an output is `Lines`); the ports of the unit's own `main` stay with `run::program`, which checks them with its inputs, so the messages of a program's ports are unchanged; a `main` nested in a block is no program's `main`, and its ports are checked as a helper's (`g() = { main() -> (out: Files) { 0 } 1 };` is rejected at 2:14) (`l01_19_whole_file_ports::files::a_helper_port_is_lines_at_l1`). `run::program` gave an output port of `main` of a type that is no port (`int`) the input-port message "is `Lines`, `Files` or `CommandLine`"; it now says "output port ... is `Lines`" (`files::ports_are_lines_or_files_at_l1`). All three tests fail on main a9330ae. Scaling: COMPLEXITY K45. |
| TD-ends | done | Interval ends of any size, replacing the `i64` end rule (docs/thresholds.md replacement 10; tradeoffs review finding 4). `End::Fin(Num)`: `store::int::Num` is one machine word while the value fits in `i64` and a big integer past it, and a value has one form. `End::from_i128`, `interval::rounded` and the outward rounding are gone; sums, products, offsets and `div` are exact across the range in both directions. `presburger::hull_from` decides each unbounded side by the recession cone (Meyer 1974) and finds a bound by doubling then bisection over `Int`, so a bound past `i64` is exact, not "no bound". `Affine::plus` and `Bounds::offset` are exact. `Landmarks` hold `Num`; a `Value::Big` literal is a landmark and a point end in the preimage. A string longer than `i64::MAX` has its exact length as its interval. The canonical serialization writes a big end as tag 3 and its decimal text. Tests changed to assert the exact result: `entries::bounds_past_i64_keep_the_box` (the last case is `x [0..=1]; y [-2..=2]; necessary`, since the points that hold are not a box), `omega::a_bound_past_i64_is_exact` (was `a_bound_past_i64_is_searched_within_i64`), `bounds::an_offset_moves_every_member` (round trips by `i64::MAX` and `i64::MIN`), `cell::a_string_too_long_for_an_i64_has_its_exact_length`. New: `l01_24_refinement_lattices::ends` (4 tests: `i64::MAX`, `i64::MIN`, one past each, sums, products and offsets crossing the boundary both ways, a bound of `2^200`), `entries::a_literal_of_2_to_the_200_is_an_exact_end`, and scaling tests `ends_in_one_word_scale_linearly` and `ends_of_2_to_the_200_scale_linearly` (COMPLEXITY.md S33). Negative control, not committed: all `Num` arithmetic through the big integer made the one-word work 1.35 to 1.46 times larger. Repair 1: a power outside `i128` is kept as base and exponent (`Num::pow`, `Word::Pow`), so the bound of `x ** 10^8` is built and compared in under a second (main gave +inf; the first TD-ends version wrote out 10^8 bits, k = 10^8 past 56 s); `Num` equality and hashing go by value (hash by residue modulo 2^61 - 1); `IntervalSet` merges adjacent pieces by `End::at_most_succ`, which does not write a power out. Tests: `ends::the_bound_of_a_large_power_is_built_and_compared_without_its_digits`, `ends::a_kept_power_equals_the_same_value_written_out`, `ends::kept_powers_order_and_hash_as_their_values_written_out`, `scaling::power_bounds_scale_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (1.1 times from k = 10^6 to 8 * 10^6; the control with powers written out, not committed, 20.8 times). `Bounds::normal` splits its borrow by field instead of copying the hull end. Repair 2: a value outside `i128` built from a power is kept as `(c * core + d) / den` (`store::int::Kept`; the core a power or a power of a floor root of a kept power), so `x ** k + 1`, `k * x ** k`, `x ** k - 1`, `div`, `mod` and gcd by a value that is not kept, and the floor and ceiling roots of a power keep the form (the verifier measured 14 s for `x ** k + 1` at k = 10^8, 17 s for the preimage of `x ** 100000000 + 1 >= 50`, and past 60 s for the cube root of 2^(10^8) and the preimage of `x ** 3 <= 2 ** 100000000`; now each is under 4 ms). Ordering compares one core by the exact sign of a linear form, a root against the power it came from without its digits, and others at doubling precision; values outside `i64` hash by their sign. Tests: `ends::kept_forms_compute_as_their_values_written_out` (156 values in kept forms against `BigInt`: order, equality, hash, sums, differences, products, `div`, `mod`, gcd, powers and roots), `ends::arithmetic_and_roots_on_a_large_power_keep_its_form` (k = 10^8, under 1 s), `l01_25_preimage_catalog::kept_ends` (2 tests, the preimages above under 1 s), `scaling::kept_ends_scale_linearly` (8.1 times from n = 200 to 1600) and `scaling::kept_arithmetic_scales_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (1.1 times from k = 10^6 + 1 to 8 times it); negative control with the kept-plus-scalar sum written out, not committed: 27.1 times, k = 10^8 past 60 s. Repair 3: a kept value is a sum of terms, `(c_1 * core_1 + ... + c_n * core_n + d) / den`, each core a product of powers of integers and of floor roots; terms are collected by core and the powers of one integer folded (`fold`), so `3 ** k + 5 ** k`, `3 ** (k + 1) - 3 ** k` (exactly `2 * 3 ** k`), `3 ** k * 5 ** (k - 1)` and `(2 ** k + 1) ** 3` keep their form (the verifier measured past 60 s and past 30 s for `zedc test` of `x ** (3 ** 100000000 + 5 ** 100000000)` and `x ** (3 ** 100000001 - 3 ** 100000000)`; now 0.01 s and 0.03 s). The approximation of a kept root is kept with its atom, so the 186-value test runs in 14.6 s of processor time (12.1 s for 156 values before). Values inside `i128` hash as themselves. Every `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment in `store/int.rs` names what it gives up, why, its trigger, its test and row S33, and a complete solution. Tests: `ends::sums_of_unrelated_large_powers_keep_their_form` (k = 10^8, under 1 s), `ends::kept_values_with_no_kept_form_are_written_out` (the written-out cases, exact), 24 more values in `kept_forms_compute_as_their_values_written_out`, and the kept scaling test extended (8.69M to 9.59M instructions, 1.1 times); negative control with sums of two kept values of different first cores written out, not committed: the scaling test and the k = 10^8 sums test past 60 s. Repair 4: each integer base is reduced to its least root (`least_root`), and two cores that differ in form are compared over a coprime base of the integers on which they differ (`Core::same`), so `x ** (3 ** e * 9 ** (e + 1) - 3 ** (3e + 2))` no longer writes out its digits (30.6 s at e = 10^6 before; `ends::equal_cores_over_bases_that_share_factors_cancel_without_their_digits` under 1 s at e = 10^6 and 8 * 10^6, `scaling::equal_cores_scale_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent`, 736527 to 826527 instructions, 1.1 times; negative control with both off, not committed: past 60 s). The hash stays by value inside `i128` and by sign outside it, which is consistent with equality; the earlier tradeoff comment proposed hashing roots by sign and big values by residue, which would break that, and is replaced; `ends::equal_values_in_different_forms_hash_alike` tests a big value equal to a kept root and kept forms equal to the values written out. Repair 5: rebased onto main 66945e0 as one commit; main's S22 transfer, the reference transfer `preimage_ref` and the S22, overflow, constant-minus and powers tests ported to exact ends (the rounding flag is gone; the S22a summary gives up on an end at or past an end of `i64`). repair 4's least root tried an integer root of all the base's bits for each prime index, cubic in the bits (verify#5: the cube of an end of 2^32768 + 3 took 69 s, 3 to 19 µs on repair 3); it now takes a 2-adic root of bits / p bits per odd prime p and checks bit count and three residues before any full power, so the cube takes 9.7 ms at 32768 bits and 89 ms at 262144. The coprime base and `exponents_over` take every power of a shared factor out at once (O(log k) divisions for multiplicity k), so `(2^n * 3) ** 1000 == 2 ** (1000 n) * 3 ** 1000` takes 0.15 ms at n = 8192 (135 ms before). The negation of the kept value 2^127 is now the big value `i128::MIN`, so it hashes as its value and `to_i128` returns it (verify#5 found the two hashes differ; the ledger item KEPT-neg-hash). Tests: `scaling::the_cube_of_a_wide_end_scales_with_its_bits`, `scaling::equality_over_a_wide_base_scales_with_its_bits`, `ends::a_wide_end_is_kept_as_a_power_of_its_least_root`, the pair `(-(2^127 kept), Big(-2^127))` in `ends::equal_values_in_different_forms_hash_alike`, and a unit test of `least_root` against trial for every base below 50000 and powers of 127-bit bases to indices up to 210; negative controls, not committed: repair 4's least root 820 times the work for 8 times the bits, the one-factor-per-step coprime base 34.5 times. Repair 6 (on main 66945e0): the wide-base equality test runs at 65536 and 524288 bits, past the size at which num-bigint changes to Burnikel-Ziegler division, which gave 26.3 times the bytes from 8192 to 65536 bits under the byte check from SCALE-bytes (17.2 times now; the one-factor-per-step control 63.9 times). With exact ends, four l01_25 expectations that main rounded or listed are now exact and not listed: `overflow::an_arm_or_a_second_input_past_max` (`y [-10..=2, 4..=10]`), both `constant_minus` tests at the ends of `i64`, and `t3_gaps` past `k = 37` in s22. `kept_ends` adds a domain unbounded on both sides, which the summary reads with the ends of `i64` standing for the infinities. The l01_39 nest of 256 functions now runs on a 256 MiB thread: the preimage still recurses on the term (T2-mid), and its frames grew about 7% (4.1 KB a level against 3.8 KB on main), past the 2 MiB of a test thread. Printing a kept value, and the other cases COMPLEXITY.md S33 lists, still write out its digits. Still `i64` after TD-ends: `Congruence` (modulus and residue), `Affine::times` (`None` past `i64`) and the end of `x ** k` past `u32`; TD-cong made them exact. |
| F44-memo | done | `zed_tests::scaling` runs the warm-up and every measured run on a new thread (`alone`, by `on_a_thread` with the stack the caller has left, read by `stacker`), so zed-kernel's per-thread memos start empty in each run and the run's bytes allocated (SCALE-bytes) are added to the measuring thread's count; a larger run whose thread counts 0 bytes is refused with its own message, so a lost count cannot pass (unit tests that allocate nothing use `check_instructions_only`, as do m0_09 `deep_scaling` "copy and free" and "the key", whose runs are `Arc` copies that allocate nothing and on main compared 0 bytes with 0); unit test `each_run_starts_with_empty_thread_memos` fails when `alone` runs on the caller's thread (1 fresh run of 5, negative control, not committed), and `a_run_alone_counts_the_bytes_its_thread_allocates` when `alone` drops its thread's bytes (0 against 16352); closures are `Fn + Sync`, so tests that held `Cell` or `RefCell` hold atomics or a `Mutex` (l01_22 with `deep_types.rs`, l01_23, l01_36, l25_08, m0_09). `l01_21` builds each size's front end before the timing and runs on a copy. Fresh ratios: l01_10 7.7, l01_21 passes; `l01_23::scaling::the_parts_scale` fails at 48.0 (12.7 M against 610 M; the old harness passes it at 7.0, negative control, not committed). Cause, by perf and a phase probe: `Part::forced` on an expression is O(atoms · size) (`is_empty` 11.0 M, 597.8 M, 36.6 G at 16, 128, 1024). T6's one-pass `forced` (merged 5b78cbd) fixes it: rebased on it, the test runs again and passes (3.67 M, 28.6 M, 229.7 M on the phase probe); `x13.rs` and the harness's own unit test now hold atomics and a `Mutex`. The gap this left, a bare thread started inside a run losing its bytes, is closed by SCALE-threads. COMPLEXITY.md F44, ledger F44-forced |
| SCALE-threads | done | The bytes a scaling run allocates now count every thread the run starts, however it starts it (bare `std::thread::spawn`, scoped, nested), as the instruction counter with `inherit` already did. Choice: per-thread counts that a new thread takes from its parent, not one count for the process, because the tests of one binary run beside each other and a process count mixes their bytes. `zed-alloc-count` defines its own `pthread_create` (std starts every thread by it; it calls the C library's, found by `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT)`), which gives the new thread an `Arc<Count>` holding its parent's; an allocation adds to its thread's count and each ancestor's (the chain is as deep as the nesting of thread starts). The main thread was not started that way, so it counts alone and the test harness's threads count on their own. `zed_alloc_count::add` is gone, since a parent sees its children's bytes as they allocate; `on_a_thread` and `alone` no longer add. Tests: `zed-alloc-count` `a_thread_counts_in_the_count_of_the_thread_that_started_it` (grandchild 4096 bytes seen in the child), `other_threads_do_not_change_this_threads_count` (measured on a sibling of 4 allocating threads), `a_thread_started_by_a_direct_call_of_pthread_create_is_counted`; `scaling` `the_bytes_of_a_bare_thread_a_run_starts_count` (on the calling thread exactly 16352 bytes, as before; on a bare thread, and on a thread that one starts, at least that; n² bytes on a bare thread fail the check). Repair 1: std records each new thread in one map of the live threads for the process (`stack_overflow/thread_info.rs`), on the new thread, and that insertion allocates 0 or about 1 KiB depending on the other threads alive, so a count holding a thread start is not deterministic beside other tests (verify 1: 16488 bytes alone, 17672 beside 8 threads; the unit test comparing a nested run with a joined one failed in 1 of 10 runs). `compare_by` now compares the bytes `alone` counts inside its thread, which hold every thread the run starts but not the start of its own thread, the least of the runs of each size; the unit test asserts only lower bounds. Test `the_bytes_of_a_run_alone_do_not_depend_on_threads_starting_beside_it` (50 runs beside 8 threads that start threads, each exactly 16352); negative control (the meter's bytes around `alone`, as before the repair, not committed): 16608 in 44 runs, 17152 in 6. The zed-alloc-count doc names this and that a test libtest runs on the main thread (when it cannot start one) does not count the threads it starts. Negative control (main's allocator with the new `scaling.rs`, not committed): that test fails at `joined >= 16352`, with 3 others that relied on `add`. Full suite with a temporary ratio log on both trees (not committed), base b0a733ff against the branch after repair 1: 330 pieces each, 0 failures; 166 of 782 bytes ratios changed, nearly all because both sizes count 232 bytes fewer (base counted the start of `alone`'s thread on the measuring thread; now `n² bytes` compares exactly 1000000 and 64000000); the largest rise is 0.12 (`printing powers` 7.96 to 8.07, `functions each calling the one before twice, alike` at 6 and 48 7.55 to 7.67); the few runs whose bytes moved by more than 232 (`the nth union built`, `a meet of cycles`, `lowering n functions each called once in place`) vary by as much between runs of one tree (three reruns); no test now fails. Gap notes removed from `scaling.rs` |
| PERF-portable | done | The scaling tests measure work on macOS and Windows, not only Linux. Linux is unchanged: user-mode instructions by `perf_event_open` (inheriting threads), else the task clock, else its own error. macOS: `proc_pid_rusage(getpid(), RUSAGE_INFO_V4)` `ri_instructions`, and `ri_cycles` when the instructions read 0, the message naming which (`unit`). Windows: `QueryProcessCycleTime` cycles. Cycles are inexact, so they get the task clock's repeats (5 runs, 3 measurements). Both counters are of the whole process, not of a thread and the threads it starts, and off Linux the bytes allocated are one count for the process, so on macOS and Windows `Meter::new` stops with its own error unless libtest runs one test at a time (`--test-threads=1`, `--test-threads 1` or `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1`). The calls are in `zed_alloc_count::os` (the workspace forbids `unsafe_code`), each behind `#[cfg(target_os)]`, with the Apple and Microsoft documentation cited; `windows-sys` 0.61.2 was already in Cargo.lock. The macOS and Windows paths are compiled and not run: `cargo check` and `cargo clippy -D warnings` pass for x86_64-apple-darwin, aarch64-apple-darwin and x86_64-pc-windows-msvc on zed-alloc-count (all targets) and on `scaling.rs` with its tests in a temporary crate (no rustup here: `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1 -Zbuild-std` on Fedora's rust-src, clang and llvm-ar). The full `zed-tests` crate does not build off Linux, before this change as after: `zed-rt/src/stack.rs` uses `libc::sysinfo`, `MAP_STACK` and `mmap` (ledger PORTABLE-rt). Tests: `a_meter_takes_the_first_counter_that_moves` (zero then moving counter gives the second, with its unit and repeats; none moving gives none), `a_meter_measures_by_any_counter_it_is_given` (linear passes, quadratic fails naming 64.0 times), `one_test_at_a_time_is_read_from_the_options_of_libtest_or_its_variable`; tests of per-thread bytes and of the task clock are Linux-only. The derived `Debug` of `Region` is replaced by one that writes the same line from a stack (100,000 deep on a 2 MiB thread, linear at 12,500 against 100,000; control: the derived one overflows). Bounded: `resolve::declare` recurses once, from an annotated or `mutable` declaration to the declaration it holds, which the grammar does not annotate again. Moved to T2-ty with the orchestrator's scope cut (ledger, 09:36), still recursive on the depth of the input: in the checker, `check/ty.rs` operations on `Ty` (`replace`, `subst`, `vars_in`, `sub`, `accepts`, `fits_sig`, `meet`, `union`, `text`, `Need::replace`, `Sig::replace`) and its derived `Clone`, `PartialEq`, `Hash`, `Display` and `Drop`, which follow the nesting of types and so of lambdas and tuples (nested lambdas overflow near 4,000; nested tuples cost about n^2.6 and nested lambdas n^2 from deep clones of `Ty`, before and after, T11-ty), and the replay of needs (`meet` to `call_found`), which starts a machine per level of nested needs; in `check/mod.rs`, `denoted_with` and `Checker::components`, which build and read `Ty`s and so cannot be tested deep before `Ty`'s own traits are; `store/product.rs` (`narrow_linear`, `narrow_node`, `exclude`, `signed`, `forward_in`, `walk`), `store/preimage.rs` (`term`, `close`, `factors`, `scan`, `class_test`), `store/presburger.rs` (`parts`, `find`, `project_each`), and the seven `on_stack` calls in the store, with `zed_kernel::on_stack` itself. Bounded and kept: `canon::inversions` (a merge sort, depth log n), `preimage::gcd` (Euclid, depth log of the value). The one-step rules `canon::binders::step`, `rename_first`, `rename` and `written` stay recursive as the specification the tests compare `canonical` against. Found: `Term`'s `Clone`, `PartialEq` and `Debug`, the kernel `Pattern`'s `Drop`, `Clone` and `PartialEq` (100,000 deep overflows in its drop glue), and the kernel `Type`'s `Drop` (a type 10,000 deep overflows when dropped) recurse (T2-types, which has since made the `Drop`s of `Term`, `Pattern` and `Type` loops). |

| TYPE-forms | merged, unpushed (6c2c0510, acc4f3ae, e3b9b5c6, 0dce3ee0, b6d0a2ee, rebased on main 34c570dd, and the commit that records this) | The bounds clause `t for X in U` (DESIGN.md sec:grammar `TypeBounds`, binding loosest) in the AST (`TypeKind::For`, `TypeBound`), resolver, elaborator, formatter and checker. Each bounded variable is bounded on the outermost signature of its clause whose arrows name it, the one a call binds it in: resolve requires every use to lie in one outermost function type of the clause, and `check::Denote` reads the type from the top down, each use recording its bound on that function type, so the cost is linear however deeply clauses nest. Diagnostics, each with its span and a note: an unbounded name is a type variable of no clause (as before), a bound on a variable the type does not hold, a variable bounded twice (in one clause or in two), a bound that is not a union of carriers, a variable used outside a function type or in two function types of its clause, and an `is` test of a bounded type (elab: a test has no call to choose a carrier). The formatter writes the clause at level 0, below arrows; the checker prints the clause after a bounded signature and `check::read::parse_type` reads printed types through the AST. Six grammar cases (393 cases in all). An unbounded name inside a clause gets a note naming the innermost clause. Passing a bounded function where a function type is expected (`ty::fits_sig`): the expected type's bounded variables are renamed apart and held rigid, each standing for every carrier of its bound, so the result does not depend on variable names and two types that use one name are not confused; a function of one carrier, `(x: int) -> int`, is the function `(x: T) -> T for T in int`. A bounded variable that no parameter holds is fixed by no argument, so it stands for the union of its bound's carriers at a call (`close`), in a fit of a passed function on both sides (`fits_in`: such a variable of the expected type is not held rigid, and one of the passed function stays unbound after its parameters), and in the check of a lambda's result (`fit_lambda`); before repair 1, `() -> T for T in int \| f64` did not fit itself and `() -> 1` was refused there. The type printer is linear (COMPLEXITY.md F66; it was quadratic in depth on main). Tests `l01_26_type_resolution::bounds` (26): parse, round trip, accepted and refused calls, one carrier, two variables, nested clauses, printed types read back, the one-tuple `(T,)` and `L .. U -> V` read by the checker, a wider bound passed where a narrower one is expected (accepted) and the reverse (refused), renamed equal bounds, a one-carrier function passed as bounded, a result-only variable (passed as itself, as a narrower function, as a lambda, and refused outside its bound or with a wider one), and functions whose parameters are functions. Only five carriers exist, so scaling is measured in the number of bounded variables and of nested clauses, not of carriers in one bound. Scaling (COMPLEXITY.md F65): `many_bounded_variables_scale`, 800 and 6,400 bounds, 8.0 times; old per-variable argument scan in `apply_one` and `refuses` (from main) 28.3 times, fixed; `nested_bounds_clauses_scale`, 400 and 3,200 nested clauses, passes; the old walk of each clause's whole type 36.0 times (26.6 at 200 and 1,600, new code 8.9). Negative controls run once, not committed. `passing_a_function_of_many_bounded_variables_scales`, 800 and 6,400, 8.3 times (linear lookup control 59.8); `printing_a_deep_function_type_scales` 7.9 times (old printer 30.5). Repair 3: `fits_in` no longer walks each signature's parameters or rewrites the expected signature at each depth; `Sig::held` is set when the signature is built and each side is read through a persistent map (`ty::Env`), so passing a function type nested d deep costs O(d) (COMPLEXITY F65: 8.1 and 8.2 times at 400 and 3,200 deep, plain and bounded; 26.6 and 59.0 times before). Not done: a bound on a declaration's own parameter types (`f(x: T for T in int) = x` is refused as standing outside a function type); a passed function whose own bounded variable occurs only under a function-typed parameter (`((x: U) -> U) -> U for U in int \| f64` where the same type with `T` is expected) is refused, soundly but needlessly, since `fits_sig` binds no variable of the outer function inside a nested function type; `Denote` does not record a bound on a variable that occurs only in a range's lower end, so that the printed type reads back; an inferred bound with the bit for values of no carrier prints as `for T in int \| other values` (`ty::mask_text`, from main), which is not user-writable syntax; the printer writes a nested bounded parameter with doubled parentheses, `((((x: T) -> T for T in int)) -> U for U in bool)`, which reads back but is redundant |

| JIT-reloc | merged and pushed (e408858b, rebased on main 43b7cdb5, and the commit that records this) | `l01_36_cranelift::table::bare_values_made_and_dropped_scale` panicked once under load in cranelift-jit `compiled_blob.rs:172`, `TryFromIntError(PosOverflow)`. That line applies `Reloc::X86PCRel4`, which non-position-independent x86-64 code emits only for the address of a symbol of the module itself: the `lea` in `codegen::Gen::root` that takes each lambda entry's address for `rt_register`. cranelift-jit's `SystemMemoryProvider` maps code one piece at a time, so the root and an entry can lie more than 2 GiB apart when other mappings land between them (inferred: another test thread's `Stack`, as large as memory; 0 of 21 runs of the l01_36 group at load 42 to 63 reproduced it by chance). Fix (`zed_native::run_in`): the JIT builds position-independent code on x86-64, as cranelift-jit's own default does. Every symbol address is then loaded through `X86GOTPCRel4`, which cranelift-jit turns into a `lea` when the symbol is within 2 GiB and otherwise into a load from an entry it adds at the end of the same allocation, and calls out of reach go through jumps added there, so no relocation depends on where the pieces lie. Rejected: one region reserved up front (`ArenaMemoryProvider`), since its size is a cap. Test: the unit test `placement::code_runs_with_its_functions_more_than_2_gib_apart` runs two lambdas, at both opt levels, with a memory provider that puts every allocation in its own 4 GiB slot; on the old flags it panics at `compiled_blob.rs:172:80` with the reported error, and passes with the fix. The 31 scaling checks of l01_36 give the same ratios with and without the change (within 0.1; e.g. n lambdas 8.05 and 8.05). Open: on other hosts the JIT stays non-position-independent (cranelift-jit refuses it there), and AArch64 takes a local function's address within 4 GiB; untested, as only x86-64 is a host now. No COMPLEXITY row: no cost changes |

PEG: `Block` gains a second alternative `\| Display` (L2, so that tuple-parameter lambdas cause no ring conflict), and `ast::Builder::body_block` (`ast/body.rs`) rejects it with `Row::DisplayInBody`, as it rejects a block whose one content is an unparenthesized lambda (`{ k -> v }`, which reads as the one-entry map). The message names the kind (set, map, record), the form whose body the brace is, the token before the brace, the top-level comma or the entry that reads as a display, and both repairs: `({ … })`, and the block reading with its value grouped, `{ (k -> v) }`, `{ (1, 2) }` or `{ x }`. Newly rejected fence: EXAMPLES.md:1024-1025, the ledger arms of sec 24, parenthesized in the spec (5996611); no corpus program is newly rejected (19 was fixed by CORPUS-parse). Tests: `l01_29_diagnostics/body_display.rs` (three full rendered messages; 15 body positions × 9 display-like braces rejected; 9 positions × 9 accepted braces and 5 direct parenthesized bodies; 7 displays outside body position), 5 new cases in `zed/grammar/cases.txt` and 2 ERROR cases that now give trees; the old-behavior cases in l01_10 lambdas, l01_11 empty_family and l02_01 blocks now expect the rejection. Spec tools: cases 392 wrong 0, check 154 parsed 0 failed, fuzz 0 containment failures, layer and ring agree. Scaling: none, O(1) per brace (COMPLEXITY.md BODY-display); the parent map for the message is built once per unit, on the first rejection. |

| L1.40d | done | T6-kinds, merged and pushed (74a76107, rebased on main c87be848, and the commit that records this). Kind-wide types as user syntax: `#_` every atom, `(..)` every tuple, and (ring 2) `{..}` every struct, `#a(..)` `#a` with any payload, `#a()` `#a` with the empty payload. Lexer: `#_` an operator (`lex::punct`), `#_x` still an atom. AST: `TypeKind::Every(Every::{Atom, Tuple, Struct})` and `TypeKind::AtomAny`; `#a()` stays `AtomApply(a, [])`. A `..` in a list with other items (`(int, ..)`, `(.., int)`, `#a(int, ..)`, `{x: int, ..}`) is a parse error with its own message `parse::DOTS` at the span of the `..` (`Error.message`). Resolve accepts `#_` and `(..)` and refuses `{..}` and `#a(..)` until L2.5, like the other ring-2 forms; `check::read::parse_type` reads all five. Checker: `Ty.set` holds the kind-wide part; `pieces`, `sub_in` (decided by the kernel), `meet`, `diff`, `contains` and `meets` handle it, so `x is (..)` narrows and `x is not (..)` leaves the rest. Kernel printer: every atom, tuple and struct print as `#_`, `(..)` and `{..}`, now exact; an atom with any payload prints `#a(..)`, so `#a(_)` is the one-tuple payload only; a payload of every tuple alone or every struct alone has no surface type and prints `#a(tuple)` or `#a(struct)`, not exact. Tests: `l01_22_type_representation::kinds` (8: format, the `..` rejection with message and span, resolve, kernel subtyping both ways, checker subtyping, print-parse round trips through both readers, two scaling tests, COMPLEXITY KINDS), `surface` (reader taught the forms; the old not-exact test now checks the exact forms and the two payload words), `zed/kinds.zed` (19 rows through `zedc test`), 15 rows in `zed/grammar/cases.txt`. Expected texts changed: `#errs`, which the checker builds with any payload (`check::ty::errs`), now prints `#errs(..)` in `l01_27_absences` (`loose`, `discard`); the counts of the step-unit tests in `l01_08_parser::cases` (408 cases), `l01_33_lowering`, `l01_34_memory`, `l01_36_cranelift` (73 rows, 84 closed terms, 146 cells) and `l01_37_oracle` (168 native runs, 16 comparisons) include the new cases and `kinds.zed`. Open: (1) the checker's subtyping compares atom payloads by the kernel set only, which holds every atom with a payload as `#a(..)` and keeps the shapes beside it, so `#a(..) <: #a(int, int)` holds in the checker (a flaw before this step; the kernel decides it); (2) `x is {..}` and `x is #a(..)` cannot be run until resolve accepts ring-2 types (L2.5), so they are tested in the kernel and through `parse_type`; (3) `first_order` and `held_function` do not see a function inside `(..)`. (4) `#errs(..)` is the type the checker holds, but the payload is a set `{E}` (row L29-print), which is narrower and has no writable type. ZED_PORT_GAPS.md "Entries from T6-kinds": the in-place writes of `meet` and `diff` (gap 11). |

| WIDE-copies | merged and pushed (074a199a, fc84ff8b, 56c364a9, rebased on main e54e4dfe, and the commit that records this) | Also MSG-operand-print. With a 2^30-bit int under `RLIMIT_AS` at its use plus 64 MiB, `1 - wide`, `wide div 3` and `wide mod 3` aborted with "memory allocation of 134217736 bytes failed". A difference flips the sign of its second operand as a flag and asks for the result's width; `mod` by a divisor of one word folds the dividend's words with no copy, and by a wider divisor folds blocks of its width; `div` asks for the dividend's width (16 times for a divisor wider than 64 bits, as repair 7 below sets) before dividing; the kept-power paths ask before each copy. Type errors from primitives and the evaluator now print each operand's type in user-writable syntax (``Lt applied to `int`, `rat` ``, ``a value of type `int` is not a function``, `Value::kind_type`) instead of its digits, so `wide < 0.5` no longer prints 2^30 bits; a bit word outside its domain says "it is negative" or "it is at least 2 ** width". The message cannot give a span: kernel terms carry none. Tests `l01_17_value_representation::in_place` (child tests under the lowered limit, small cases, a scaling test at power 1). COMPLEXITY.md K30 repair 6. Open: `Rat::mul` and `Rat::div` stay unchecked for callers outside the evaluator. Repair 7 (review): the asks were below measured peaks, so wide divisions and a wide `mod` aborted under a limit; `div` by a wide divisor now asks 16 times the dividend (peak 15.0), `rem` 24 times the divisor (peak 21.3), the modular-power step 32 times (peak 25.2), the plain sum, `exact_div` and `aligned` 16 times (peaks 8.8, 10.9, 10.7); refusal and answer tests at margins between the old and new asks, negative control aborts on the old ones; COMPLEXITY.md K30 repair 7. Repair 8 (verify#2): the asks of `rem_built`'s modular-power step, `exact_div` and `aligned` carry Complexity tradeoff comments, and the comments of `rem_built` and `div_euclid` no longer say no input is refused for its size; child tests `a_kept_power_modulo_a_wide_number_is_refused_below_its_ask` (margin 28 times the divisor, between the measured peak and the ask) and `an_exact_quotient_of_a_wide_number_is_refused_when_memory_is_short`; COMPLEXITY.md K30 repair 8. |

(1) A literal compared by `==` with an inferred parameter, or at an open type variable, followed nothing and stayed int, so `isOne(1.0)` was false: it now follows the class's carrier (`Info::follows`, rewritten by the elaborator to a match on f64 and rat), and a literal that is no value of that carrier is unequal (`reading == 0.5` at int is false). A literal argument at an open type variable keeps its own carrier. (2) An int literal at `f64 \| String` or `f64 \| bool` now adopts f64. (3) Int literals in a tuple adopt the components' carriers: `sum((x, 2))` with x: f64 and `t((1, 2))` at `(f64, f64)`, nested too. DESIGN L1.26 said only that a literal adopts the carrier "an operator or a parameter expects"; at a union with two numeric carriers no one carrier is expected, so the item decides, and the spec sentence (wt/lit-adopt-spec) states: the literal keeps its own carrier (int, or f64 for a decimal) when the type holds it (`1` at `int \| f64` is int, `0.5` at `int \| rat` is rat, its one adoptable carrier) and is rejected otherwise (`1` at `f64 \| rat`: "adopts no one carrier", naming `1.0` and `1r`). The preimage catalog (`Node::Follow`) and the reduced product (`plain_comparison`, `plain_test`) read a following literal; `preimage_ref` has no Follow node and is unchanged (ledger LIT-follow-tuple notes it). `sum` reads its components in written order. Three quadratic paths a deep tuple reached were fixed: the data check on each tuple level (`tuple_step`, `data` by reference), the `seen` map keeping a cloned type per tuple level (a tuple written out keeps none; `Ck::tuple_seen` rebuilds it for a derivation), and `equal` cloning each side (`Side` borrows). Control on base 29d86d1e: deep d=64 0.18 s, d=512 13.8 s; now 15 ms and 128 ms. Tests: `l01_26_type_resolution::literals` (11, with the `zed/readings.zed` unit of 18 rows read as sensor readings, and scaling at n and 8n over width and depth), one each in l01_17 equality, l01_29 derivations and batch (batch and single-file checking agree on the `f64 \| rat` rejection); corpus counts in l01_33/34/36/37 moved by the new rows. Full suite `tools/run_tests.sh 2` (load 24 to 58 on 32 cores): 333 pieces, 2 failed: l01_07_fences, failing on base 29d86d1e too, and l01_29 batch on a placeholder message since filled in and rerun. Follow-up LIT-follow-tuple: a literal inside a tuple compared with a class component does not follow the class's carrier. Repair #1 (verifier's defects): (a) the reduced product read a following comparison by forwarding the compared value again per test, 2^d on nested following comparisons (6.19 s at d = 12); now the value is found once per test and only a name at an integer is rewritten (chain of 48: 731 ms against 699 ms plain); tests `l01_39_reduced_product::follow` (5, with scaling at n and 8n). (b) `(1, 2)` at `(f64, f64) \| (String, f64)` was rejected because two shapes have length 2; a literal component now adopts the one carrier that component holds across the shapes of its length, nested too (spec clause in wt/lit-adopt-spec dd50abd); tests in `l01_26_type_resolution::literals` and a batch unit in l01_29. (c) "adopts no one carrier" names the carriers in printed order (`rat and f64`, `1r` or `1.0`). Found, not fixed: the plain nested-condition chain in `product::arms` is about d^2.4 (COMPLEXITY F70, item PRODUCT-nested-chain). Repair #2 (verifier's defects): (a) `x == 1` with `x: f64 \| rat` was accepted and false at `1.0` and `1r`: `Ck::follow` now decides by `carriers_in`, the rule of a parameter, so it is rejected ("adopts no one carrier", naming `1r` and `1.0`), and `x == 1` at `int \| f64` keeps int (so `f(1.0)` is false there, as the spec says). (b) `p == (1, 2)` with `p: (f64, f64) \| (String, f64)` was false at `(1.0, 2.0)`: `==` paired components only at exactly one tuple shape; `sides` now keeps each component's types across every shape of the length, as `adopted` does. (c) A tuple component at two carriers, neither its own (`(1, "a")` at `(f64, String) \| (rat, String)`), now gets the "adopts no one carrier" message from `adopted`, the same as a bare literal, instead of the generic one; the separate bare-literal check at the parameter became dead and was removed. Spec clause for `==` in wt/lit-adopt-spec b1719c5. Tests: two in `l01_26_type_resolution::literals` (15 tests now), `==` added to the shapes scaling test, two batch units in l01_29. Still as documented: `outer2(x) = { g(positional y) = y == x; g(1) }` is false at `1.0`, because `g`'s parameter is an open variable and a literal argument there keeps its own carrier; the same program with `x: f64` declared agrees. |
| LIT-deep-instr | merged and pushed (4b2418cc, rebased on main a6a7c39b, and the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md F69 (repair LIT-deep-instr). The deep scaling test of literal adoption timed the check of the whole program, which spends about 357,000 instructions a level outside `==`, so an O(d^2) walk in `sides` that allocates nothing passed it. New test `l01_26_type_resolution::literals::pairing_the_sides_of_a_deep_comparison_scales` times `==`'s pairing alone (`Ck::pair`, split out of `Ck::equal`; operands built before timing by the hidden `check::Operands`), 2,048 and 16,384 deep: 8.0x instructions; the O(d^2) control fails it at 37.3x (limit 24), run once and not committed. The pairing costs about 106,000 instructions a level, mostly `class_of_ty` of a literal's carrier (a constant per level, not changed here). |
| TEST-fence-lines | merged and pushed (10595c64, rebased on main 173ff21a, and the commit that records this) | The fences' expected outputs are keyed by section, not by DESIGN line. `sync` writes in each fence's header its section (the label of the nearest labeled heading above it, `sec:remap_law`, or else the nearest heading's text, as in EXAMPLES.md's `3. Numbers`) and its place among that section's fences; `zed_tests::fences::DocFence::key` reads them (`DESIGN.md [sec:remap_law] 5`), and `at` keeps the line for messages. The l01_07 snapshots `every_design_fence_lexes`, `every_examples_fence_lexes` and `expected_marks` (was `golden_marks`) and the expected list of l01_21 use the key; regenerated once, they hold the same 47 + 107 blocks with the same tokens in the same order and the same 19 marks. `l01_07_fences::keys` checks the keying on synthetic specs run through `sync`: lines added above a fence change no expected output, a fence added to one section changes no other section's keys, and keying scales (COMPLEXITY F71). A merge that moves DESIGN.md lines no longer needs `INSTA_UPDATE` on l01_07; it still runs `sync`, since the copies record lines |
| ISSUE1-else-atoms | merged and pushed (7e5657d9, d4492e23, ddd6fb4a, 3a5d6f07, 2f63560d, f9fb8e6b, 0ba6b058, 52b246ed, 253e00bb, c3044ee8, rebased on main 62d5c21a, and the commit that records this; spec d5fe842, 1c37182, 22bd599, ef1ec9e) | An undeclared parameter used as the operand of `else`, or tested by `is #a`, was bounded to carriers only, so a loose atom passed the call by (rule 6 of sec:type_resolution) and the body's handling of it never ran: `r(positional line) = trim(line else "")` with `r(#notFound)` gave `#notFound`. Now the resolver records each `else` and `is` whose operand is a parameter's name (`Resolution::handles`, `resolve::Handle`), and the checker joins the atoms they handle per parameter (`check::handled`): every loose atom for `p else d`, `#a` with any payload for `p else #a -> d`, `#a` for `p is #a`. The parameter's type in the body and in its signature is its class's type joined with those atoms (`param_ty`, `handled_tys`, at `InferEnd` and `LambdaEnd`); a class that already holds every value stays a type variable, and the class's own type is unchanged, so `else` still strips what it strips. The issue's function is `(String \| #_) -> String`, `(line else 0) + 1` is `(int \| #_) -> int`, `add(a, b) = (a else b) + b` gives `a` the atoms beside its number carrier. A name bound to a parameter, `s = line` (also through a chain, `t = s; s = line`), handles atoms for it: the resolver records each such binding (`Resolution::aliases`) and `check::handled` joins the binders in a union-find before joining the atoms per class; a name bound to a computed value (`s = trim(line)`) handles nothing for `line`. The atoms are recorded per parameter in the checker, not held on the union-find classes of the type checker as decided: class-held atoms gave `(line else 0) + 1` the type `(int \| #_) -> int \| #_`. Printed signatures (T6-kinds syntax, each read back by `check::read::parse_type`): the issue's rows 1, 2 and 5 are `(_) -> "s" \| #absent`, `(_) -> "" \| #absent \| _`, `(_) -> "" \| _` (an unbounded type variable printed `_`, as PRINT-tyvars prints it); row 3 is `(String \| #_) -> String \| #absent` (not `#notFound`, since the body also has `line else ""`, as the rule gives); rows 4 and 6 are `(String \| #_) -> String`. Origin notes for a binding and a narrowing now print their type only when a message shows them; formatting it eagerly was quadratic in a chain of names (45.9 times the bytes at 8n). Tests: `l01_26_type_resolution::handled` (15, signatures by subtyping and the exact printed forms), `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (7, values of the issue's rows and the issue's program run whole), the two scaling tests (COMPLEXITY.md F74). Red before green: with the atoms not recorded, 13 of the first 19 fail; with aliases not recorded, 4 fail. Behavior change: `if line is #notFound {""} else {trim(line)}` called with a line of stdin ran before and is now rejected, since the parameter is `String \| #notFound`, as a declared parameter of that type is; the message also lists `#notFound` among the atoms `r` does not take, which is wrong and belongs to CLASS-atoms (rule 6 is all-or-nothing per argument). No existing expected printed signature changed. Repair 2 (verify 2, blocking 1): with one arrow `(String \| #_) -> String \| #_`, a body that handles the atoms and also passes them on (`if (line else "") == "" { "empty" } else { trim(line) }`, and the `bool` and `int` forms `{ d = c else false; if c {..} else {..} }`, `{ d = n else 0; if n > 3 {..} }`) gave `r(" a ")` a result a `Lines` port refused; those programs ran on main. Now such a function has two arrows, `((String) -> String) & ((String \| #_) -> String \| #_)`: the first, for an argument with no atom, gives the loose atoms that reach the result from elsewhere than the parameter (`Ck::outside`, a set per expression computed beside `unnamed`, F75); the second takes the handled atoms. There is one arrow when no atom of the parameter reaches the result, or when the parameter's class holds other values. A call selects the first arrow that takes its arguments' atoms (`Ck::first_taking`, then `apply_one` on that arrow); an argument whose type is a class of the function being inferred takes the first arrow, so `g(positional x) = r(x)` is `(String) -> String` and an atom passed to `g` passes it by, as on main; a variable of an enclosing signature, or a dynamic type, takes the second. The reader of printed types read `(A) & (B)` of two function types as `A` alone (`Ty::meet` keeps the first side's functions, a pre-existing flaw); an intersection of function types of one arity now reads as one signature of all their arrows, so the printed two-arrow signatures read back. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (+3: the verifier's three programs run at a `Lines` port, arrow selection with a function and a lambda, the wrapper `g`), `l01_26_type_resolution::handled` (+2: the printed two-arrow signatures read back, an atom of the body's own stays on the first arrow), `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_chain_of_atoms_from_elsewhere_than_the_parameter_scales`; the two older scaling tests now name the arrow they read. Verify 2 blocking 2 (scaling tests panic off Linux, `tests/src/scaling.rs:138`) is pre-existing and filed as PERF-portable, not fixed here. Also: `perf-event2` is a Linux-only dependency, and `zed_tests::scaling` builds off Linux with no counter (checked by clippy with the condition switched to macOS, not by a macOS build). Open: the payload forms `is #e(int)` and `else #e(v) -> d` are not read by the resolver; they cannot be written before L2.4 and L2.5, which must add them (ISSUE1-payload); printing of `#_` (ISSUE1-print) is done here, by T6-kinds; a type variable with `else` (`(line else 0) + bump` is `(T, bump: int) -> int` and fails at run time on a string) is present on main and recorded as ISSUE1-tvar-else. Repair 3 (verify 3, blocking): a function that handles its parameter's atoms and passes it to a two-arrow function (`g(positional x) = if (x else "") == "-" { "dash" } else { r(x) }`) got one arrow `(String \| #_) -> String \| #_`, because `outside_of` took the result atoms of every arrow of the callee; `g(" a ")` at a `Lines` port was rejected, and ran on main. Now the call contributes only the result of the arrow its arguments' outside atoms select (`Ck::outside_arrow`, `Ck::taking`, shared with `first_taking`), so `g` has the first arrow `(String) -> String`. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (+1, two forms of `g` run at a `Lines` port) and `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::a_chain_of_functions_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scales` (signatures of a chain of three, and scaling, F75); both fail with the fix disabled. Not done here: on the second arrow the result is `#_`, not the atoms the argument holds (less precise, sound) Repair 4 (verify 4, blocking 1): the method form `g(positional x) = if (x else "") == "-" { "dash" } else { x.r() }` gave `g` one arrow, and `g(" a ")` at a `Lines` port was rejected; so were `x?.r()`, `x.r().r()`, `f = r; f(x)` and a local lambda called on `x`, all of which ran on main. `outside_of` took the arrow selection only for a call whose callee is the name of a declared function, and `outside_arrow` placed no receiver. Now `Ck::called` resolves the callee as `call` and `member_end` do (a name, or a member `x.r` / `x?.r`, bound to one binder whose type is one function, declared or a value) and gives the receiver, which `outside_arrow` puts in the first parameter, as `call_sig` does (with no atom for `?.`, which passes the receiver's atoms by), and which `outside_of` passes on as a part. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled::a_method_call_passes_its_receiver_as_the_first_argument` (8 programs; red before green: the four method forms with the arrow selection failed on the branch before the fix, `method_handles_then_passes_on` first), two scaling tests (F75). Left open, non-blocking: the second arrow's result holds `#_` rather than the argument's own atoms. Repair 5 (verify 5, blocking): a method call without parentheses, `x.r`, `x.r.r` and `{ d = x else ""; x.r }`, gave `g` one arrow, and `g(" a ")` at a `Lines` port was rejected; so were `ap(r, x)` through a higher-order `ap(positional f, positional v) = f(v)` and the computed callee `(if true { r } else { r })(x)`, all of which ran on main. `outside_arrow` read the arrow selection from the callee as written, and these callees are not among those it reads. Now each call inside a function with a widened parameter records the loose atoms it gives from outside (`Ck::tried`): when an argument holds a loose atom, the call is applied once more with each such argument cut to its atoms from outside (`Ck::outside_args`, `Ck::try_outside`, an undo-log trial like those of `Ck::repairs`), and the trial's result atoms are recorded; otherwise the call records its own result atoms. A call of a value of several functions joins the records of each; a call of a type variable or a dynamic value records unknown, so all its atoms. The check of a need replayed at a call (`replaying`) records nothing, since its calls are not in the text. `outside_of` takes the record for a call or a member; `outside_arrow` is gone. A bare member `x.trim` of a prelude word takes the word's rule, as `trim(x)` does. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled::a_method_call_passes_its_receiver_as_the_first_argument` (+4: `x.r`, `x.r.r`, `x.r` after a binding, `x.trim`) and `a_function_value_called_with_a_handled_parameter_gives_a_string_for_a_string` (new, 3: `ap(r, x)`, the computed callee, a wrapper `g` over `ap` that handles nothing); red before green: both tests failed at their new rows on the branch before the fix. Scaling: `many_calls_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scale` covers the three new call forms, and `a_chain_of_method_calls_without_parentheses_scales` is new (F75). Not fixed, present on the branch since its first commit and not on main: inside a handler, a lambda passed through a higher-order function, `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), x)`, is rejected ("`f` does not take (#_)"), since a need checked at a call has no argument span and so no rule 6 pass-by; main ran it because there `x` held no atom, and main rejects the same call on `stdin[2]` directly. Recorded as ISSUE1-lambda-need. Repair 6 (verify 6, blocking 1 and 2): rule 6 passed an argument's loose atoms by only when every arrow refused all of them, so a body that tests for one atom, `if line is #notFound { "nf" } else { trim(line) }` (the parameter `String \| #notFound`), and `trim(line else "")` were rejected when called with `stdin[2]` at a `Lines` port, whose element may also be other atoms; a declared parameter `(positional line: String \| #notFound)` was rejected the same way, also on main. Now pass-by is per atom: the atoms every arrow omits pass by, the rest stay in the argument and run the body, and `Info::passes` records the set each argument passes (`Ck::refused`, `Ck::pass_at`; the elaborator tests the argument with `is` that set before the call, `elab::walk::pass_end`). Blocking 2 (ISSUE1-lambda-need): `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), x)` inside a handler, alone or after `d = x else ""`, was rejected on the branch and ran on main. Of the two choices, (a) pass the refused atoms by at the higher-order call and (b) keep `x` unwidened, this repair takes (a), which follows rule 6 per atom: a need replayed at a call records the atoms the lambda refuses (`Ck::need_passed`), and the call that gave the lambda passes them by at the argument whose parameter holds the need's type variables (`Ck::pass_needs`); an atom that no argument of the call gives is still rejected (`ap(f) = f(#foo)`: "`f` does not take (#foo)"), and a need checked at a function's own body with no such call is rejected as before. Behavior change: `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), stdin[2])` now runs and gives the atom (main rejected it). Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (+4: the is-only, pattern-else and declared bodies at a `Lines` port, an omitted atom passing by beside a tested one, four lambda forms through `ap` and `ap2`, the rejected `f(#foo)`); red before green: on the branch before the fix the first five probe programs were rejected (and the declared one and the direct `ap` on main). Scaling: `many_calls_passing_some_atoms_of_their_argument_by_scale` and `a_chain_of_functions_each_testing_an_atom_of_its_own_scales` (F76). Still open, non-blocking: the second arrow's result holds `#_` rather than the argument's own atoms. |

| AND-fns | merged and pushed (240bfcc6, 42b40566, cb992e04, rebased on main 6ba12c8a, and the commit that records this; spec 5be8ce5, e7b9d75) | COMPLEXITY.md F82. `((x: int) -> String) & ((x: f64) -> String)` read back by `parse_type` as one signature of two arrows, as DESIGN.md sec:type_language point (2) says ("it is notation for that list"); before, `Denote` met the two function types as a meet, and the printed overload did not read back as itself. The rule the spec branch adds: the arrows share one parameter list (count, and the name of each parameter a call names; a positional or unnamed parameter's name is not compared) and one set of bounds; `resolve` and `parse_type` reject an intersection of function types that differ there, two operands that bound one variable by different carriers, and a bounds clause around an intersection that is an operand of another. An overload in parentheses inside another, `(A & B) & C`, is read as `A & B & C` (repair 2: before, a nested intersection was joined by arity alone, so a differing parameter name was dropped without an error, and a second bound of one name replaced the first). A mixed intersection (`int & f`) keeps the meet. A slot written as an overload takes each arrow (`l01_26_type_resolution::fits`). `fits_in` tries an equal arrow first by hash; arrows that differ still cost n·m (open: AND-fns-fit). Found, not changed: `inc(x) = x + 1` passed to an overload slot is read as `(x: int) -> int` and rejected, as on main |

## Decisions made

- Toolchain: Fedora's rustc and cargo 1.99.1, edition 2024. CI uses the stable toolchain.
- Cargo's target directory is `build/cargo`, set in `.cargo/config.toml`.
- Language tests: `tests/steps/<step>/`, all in one test binary. Unit tests of internal
  functions: `#[cfg(test)]` beside the code.
- Fences: CI must not read the spec repository (the author's instruction, 2026-10-29).
  The `sync` tool, run by hand, copies the fences into `zed/fences/design.md` and
  `examples.md`, each under a header with its source line and layer; `sync --check` exits
  1 when a copy is stale, and the step test `the_copies_match_the_spec_where_it_is_present`
  compares the texts where `../zed` exists (it passes without checking in CI).
- No Python (the author's instruction, 2026-10-31): the repositories hold Rust and Zed only, plus
  `tools/dagview` in TypeScript. The tools are the
  binaries of the crate `crates/zed-tools`, each taking the spec directory as its first
  argument, run as `cargo run -q -p zed-tools --bin TOOL -- ../zed ...`: `check`, `cases`,
  `fences`, `fuzz`, `layer`, `ring`, `audit`, `sync`, `progress`, and `plan`, which checks
  `docs/plan_dag.md` and takes no spec directory. They share the grammar rules
  and the engine of `zedc::parse`. The corpus references are the crate `zed/corpus/ref`
  (`ref NN INPUT`, `ref --write`), which depends on no compiler crate; the step test
  `m0_04_corpus_references` compares every `.out` with it in the local suite, so CI needs no
  other language.
- Reference 13 stores only the nodes on edges, in a hash map from the node number to a slot in
  the parent and size arrays, so input of 20 bytes can name a node of any size; components are
  the node count less the merges. Reference 14 is O(n log n) by a Delaunay triangulation in exact
  integers, and its O(n²) form is the test oracle (COMPLEXITY.md F39, plan node P14-MST, done).
  Of two shortest digit strings equally near a float, printing takes the larger magnitude:
  Rust's `{:e}` does (8,000,000 ties sampled in [2^50, 2^51) all rounded up;
  `core::num::flt2dec::strategy::dragon` rounds up when twice the remainder is at least the
  scale), and the spec now says so (sec:reduction).
- The tools (DART-RS). A hole failure gives the hole parser's position in the hole's own tokens.
  `progress` with no sec:steps refuses with exit status 1. Identifiers follow the
  `unicode-ident` crate, as the compiler does. A duplicate rule across the syntax and
  documentation grammars is an error, `audit` passes on the exit status of `layer`, a crash
  exits 1, `check` reads fences from its spec argument, and `fuzz` computes depths by Knuth's
  generalization of Dijkstra's algorithm. `sync` copies `lexical.peg` and `desugar.txt`, and not
  `cases.txt` or `leftrec.txt`. A spec with fewer than three `peg` fences is refused by every
  tool. A failure is a one-line message, not a stack trace.
- The references: on a cycle, 15 and 27 give an error. The second rewrite (04, 07, 15, 27, 31
  and 02, 05, 16) agrees with the first on 13800 random and mutated inputs and 1800 well-formed
  typechecker inputs.
- No reference recurses on its input now, and the tools parse with the iterative machine of
  `zedc::parse`, generic over its input, so `on_large_stack` is gone from both crates and a deep
  program runs through `ring`, `layer` and `check` on a 2 MiB stack. The tools still recurse over
  the grammar's text (`Reader`, `Expr`'s `Drop` and `Clone`, `fuzz`'s walks of rules), which is
  bounded by the nesting of the spec's own rules, not by a program. A `%` in the grammar copies
  its operand, so nested `%` doubles per level; the spec does not nest it. Reference 13 allocates by a node number in its input, and 16 and 26
  loop over a tick or step count in their input: their cost follows a value, not a
  length. References 12, 14 and 15 no longer repeat their corpus programs' algorithms: 12 is one
  pass with a stack, O(n); 14 keeps a distance matrix and each cluster's nearest, O(n²); 15 applies
  an edit only to the cells that depend on it, by change in value. On a count of clusters near -1e24, 14
  gives an error, where the earlier version panicked.
  Step rows and notes above that name a `.py` file record what was built at the time.
- The reference evaluator computes integers in `i64` and widens to a big integer instead of
  overflowing; a number past 2^20 bits is its `Overflow` error (L1.17). It has no fuel unless
  a caller sets one (docs/thresholds.md 8), and no limit on nested calls: its frames are on the heap (T2-eval). A divergent program
  at run time therefore ends only when time or memory does: `w(positional f) = f(f)` runs
  forever in constant space, and `w(positional f) = 1 + f(f)` grows until an allocation is
  refused (message, status 134) or the system kills it (no message). The operations on a
  `Value` keep their depth on the heap as well (K22); reading a variable still copies its
  `Value` (K21, T14-value).
- Coverage runs in CI (`cargo llvm-cov`, job `coverage`), not locally: this machine has no
  LLVM tools, and installing them is a system package change.
- Workspace lints: `missing_docs` warns, `unsafe_code` is forbidden. `zed-native` denies it
  instead and allows it at the two places that call and free JIT code; `zed-rt` needs none.

- No step waits for a person (the author's instruction, 2026-10-29; spec commit 827385b).
  M0(d) is now a prediction suite kept as tests, the M0(e) postmortems run alongside and gate
  nothing, and the corpus programs are corrected to fit the language rather than the reverse.
  When the spec is unclear, I choose, record the choice in DESIGN.md and here, and continue.

- `@exact` (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 8a): an opt-in for the member-by-member answer
  on ranges, never listed. An exact scope keeps the algebraic bounds and the written
  constraints, and is bottom exactly when no point of the bounded integer names meets them:
  each group of constraints sharing names is searched for one point (`preimage::witness`,
  branch and prune after Granger's class arithmetic), and the point is kept and checked
  again before any new search. A question about the exact set is a test assumed in the scope.
  No size limit, by default or otherwise (a default would be a threshold). Off by default;
  the annotation's syntax lands with L15.
- Enumeration (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 8): only sets the source lists are
  enumerated, each evaluation admitted by `stage::ends` and run with no fuel; ranges are left
  to the interval, congruence and relation domains. The M0.10 harness's instances (integers
  `-8..=8`, nine floats, 4,096 bindings) are test fixtures in `zed_tests::instances`, not a
  rule of Zed; the rung 3 and rung 6 budgets, never read by the compiler, were deleted.
- M0.5 values (DESIGN.md M0.5): widening moves a bound to the next landmark (a literal
  k as k − 1, k, k + 1) or to ±∞, at every head change, with no delay and no cap; narrowing
  moves a bound back to an old endpoint or a landmark and lets an infinite end take a bound
  once; phase 2 runs until a round changes nothing; a finite set widens to ⊤ at a member
  that is not a landmark (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 3); no constant bounds pieces or
  listings (3c): forward revision caps the smaller operand to ⌈(k + m)/k⌉ pieces and each
  sign region of the image to the operands' largest region, `x mod c` lists its first and
  last periods and one piece between, and the reduction lists an interval only at two
  members a piece; phase 1 rises, phase 2 and the reduction fall, asserted in test builds; Presburger relations
  have no size limit (replacement 4). Rule 1 of sec:constraints now says phase 1 is Bourdoncle's recursive
  strategy and only phase 2 runs in rounds; the sec:domains example is the tested three-cell
  loop. `flat(elems, top:)` is defined there (bottom `#empty`).
- M0.6 (DESIGN.md M0.6): the scalar sums devices as if run in sequence, an upper bound on
  the running time; L24 may refine the estimate but must keep it monotone in the frontier's
  quantities. The frontier is per device, because the scalar is not monotone in plan totals
  (work moved to a slower device costs more).
- M0.6a (DESIGN.md M0.6a): the footprint step M0.6 summed is now the peak of the schedule;
  retained bytes count until the schedule ends (COMPLEXITY.md C27), since `Region` carries no
  last reader. L3.23 and L24.5 fill `retained`.
- M0.7 (DESIGN.md M0.7): the canonicalizer runs over kernel terms now and moves onto ZIR at
  L1.32. A canonical binder name is NUL then its level, which no source name can contain. The
  definition's own name, port names, atom names and catalog witnesses are part of the
  interface and so of the address. Renaming one binder per pass is O(n²) in the binders;
  L1.32 may do it in one pass if it gives the same normal form.
- M0.11 (DESIGN.md M0.11): an axiom is per arrow; a law every arrow of a name must have is
  listed at each kernel carrier. `+` at `f64` has identity -0.0, not 0.0. `min` and `max`
  have no identity. A unit has no version number: its address covers its exported
  declarations only, so its path and unused local declarations do not change it. The
  reference evaluator sets no memory limit; a step is a call, a `fix` round or a `gen` step.
  `DEFAULT_FUEL` was removed (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 8); the evaluator has no fuel
  unless a caller sets one. AXIOMS-order: a law relating two predicates is `Implies(q)` or
  `Composes(q, r)`, keyed by the first operator, so L3.8 finds the composition of links
  `a op b` and `b q c` as the axiom of `op` with law `Composes(q, _)`; transitivity stays
  `Transitive`, not `Composes(op, op)`. AXIOMS-gaps: `!=` at `f64` is only `Symmetric`
  (`NaN != NaN`), as DESIGN M0.11 says since c4f4680; `String` order is by code point, which
  `str::cmp` gives as UTF-8 byte order. AXIOMS-bool: `==` and `!=` at `bool` have the laws
  of `int` and `String`, as DESIGN M0.11 gives them at every carrier but `f64`. AXIOMS-chain-string:
  the order chain compositions hold at `String` as at `int`, keyed the same way. BIT-axioms:
  the bit words have no width, so their laws do not wrap; `shr` is floor division, and the
  harness checks the word operators by table on every width 1 to 8 rather than on rung 6.
- L1.1 (DESIGN.md sec:lexical): a leading byte order mark is rejected, like a file not in NFC;
  a lone `\r` is a line break. A U+FEFF after the start is the lexer's to reject. Dependency:
  `unicode-normalization`.
- L1.2 (DESIGN.md sec:lexical): mixed script is the complement of UTS #39 Highly Restrictive,
  so Greek with Latin (`ΔT`) is rejected. Dependencies: `unicode-ident`, `unicode-script`.
- L1.3: an integer token keeps its base and digits, not a value; `0755` is decimal, as the
  grammar reads it.
- L1.4: a float or rational token keeps its decimal text, not a value, so `1e400` lexes and its
  range is checked where its type is known; `0x10r` is rejected because `Rational` takes only
  decimal digits.
- L1.5: `"""` stripping applies only when both delimiters are on their own lines (DESIGN,
  sec:lexical, string row); a `"` string has no line break, its holes included, and a `//`
  comment in such a hole is its own error. An empty string is `Str([])`.
- L1.6: doc comments are kept outside the token stream, since the grammar discards every
  comment; the parser attaches them by span.
- L1.7: a fence's `// layer Lk` label may be on any whole comment line, since a fence's first
  line is usually its `// [d4]` header; the spec's `layer.label_of` reads only a program's first
  line, which gave EXAMPLES.md:432 layer L1 under its `// layer L2`. `sync.py` reads every line.
- L1.7: where a variant replaces a line instead of adding one (the loop variants of the fence
  at DESIGN.md:2420, a second `q =` or `rows =`), its fixture replaces that line, so the fixture has one
  error. The two column variants of the `orders` store edit its field type and put the ✗ there.
- L1.7: `…` is the name `…` (sec:grammar, "Documentation notation"); Part G said "a hole, not a
  token", now aligned to sec:grammar in DESIGN.md.
- TEST-fence-lines: a fence's section is the nearest heading above it with a `(sec:…)` label,
  not the nearest heading of any kind, so a fence under an unlabeled `###` of DESIGN.md (the
  remap law's "The legal moves") counts in its labeled section; EXAMPLES.md has no labels, so
  there the heading's text names the section. Two sections of one name count on from each other,
  so every key is distinct (`every_fence_key_is_its_own`).
- L1.8: the parser runs the copied grammar instead of a hand-written precedence climber, so
  its trees are `check.py`'s by construction and the grammar cannot drift from the spec
  unnoticed (DESIGN.md L1.8 now says so). It parses every form of every layer at L1; steps
  L1.9–L1.12 add the elaboration of their forms, and the elaborator rejects a node whose
  alternative's layer (`Tree::layer`) is above the program's.
- L1.8: a variant of a documentation-notation fence is parsed as a `DocFence`, as its fence
  is (`exec : … -> Rows | #sqlError` is a signature without its `;`).
- L1.9: the expression syntax tree is built from the parse tree after parsing, not by the
  parser, which only records rule, alternative and tokens. Each later step adds its forms to
  `zedc::ast`, and the fence test's counts of built and later chains move with them. `**` is
  built here with the other operators of the L1.8 ladder, ahead of its L1.10 listing. The
  uniform call stays a call of a member until resolution (L1.14, L1.16).
- L1.10: the type expressions are built here, ahead of their L1.12 listing, since `is T` and a
  lambda's `x: T` are written with them; L1.12 still adds the declarations that use them. A
  destructuring parameter `((s, c)) -> …` and patterns other than atom patterns are L2.
- L1.11: a block in expression position must export; a declaration's body block need not
  (`Counter.tick() { n' = n + 1; }`), and L1.12 builds those. Statement order has no
  meaning, so a `return` may precede a binding. A name bound twice in one block is left to
  name resolution (L1.14).
- L1.12: a unit's top level holds declarations and pushes, not an export, so `return` there
  is an error. A function's several `test` tables are one list of cases in written order.
  The result form `f(x) -> T = …` is L10 by the grammar and is not built at L1.
- L1.13: the formatter's layout is not given in full by DESIGN.md, so these choices are
  recorded there: two-space indentation; no line breaking by width (a known limit, revisit
  when the corpus has long lines); a block on one line only when it holds just a one-line
  export and no comment; a function body always on several lines; several `test` tables
  joined into one; a comment inside a statement but outside its blocks moved to the end of
  the statement. A block that is an operand opens with a statement at L1, so its export is
  written `return e;`. The builder stores a block's export last, so "the same AST" holds
  exactly.
- L1.14: where DESIGN.md is silent, recorded there: a function's or lambda's parameters and
  output ports share one scope with its body block's statements; a parameter's type and
  default see the parameters but not the body; `test` cases are read around the function;
  an `else` pattern's `f:` fields are in scope in its default only; functions of one name
  whose written parameter types differ form an overload set; a member `x.f` is recorded only
  when `f` is bound in the unit (fields are told apart at L1.16); the L1 prelude words are the
  numeric casts and `abs`, `len`, `lower`, `upper`, `trim`, `sum` (`count` takes a family, so
  L3); the L1 type names are `int`, `rat`, `f64`, `bool`, `String`, `Lines`, `Files`.
- L1.15: the kernel already had all seven generators (M0.9); "whole" adds the `ev` of
  `fold(F, op, ev)` and `fix(F0, step, ev)`. The record maps a property name to a grade; a
  property absent is Unknown, and a derived property is stored at the meet of its premises'
  grades (the rule and premises are the property tracker's), so the tags are Axiom,
  CheckedExhaustive, Proved, Bounded, Obligated, Assumed, and only the first three are proof
  grade. The record is part of the term, so it is serialized into the address, and VERSION is
  now 2. The printer's notation: binary primitives infix and always parenthesized, other
  primitives as functions, a generator's named parts as `depth: 2`, `witness: residue`,
  `join: union`, `guard: before`, `definedness`, the record as `ev: { associative: axiom }`;
  ports as `port.read(stdin)` and `port.push(stdout, e)`. A term that does not fit puts each
  part of its outermost list on its own line, two spaces in, and an `if` breaks both branches
  together.
- L1.16: `++` is its own kernel primitive `Concat`, not `Add`, since `+` and `++` are distinct
  operators with different laws (DESIGN.md, Property tracking); `**` is `Pow`. Every binder
  gets a kernel name unique in the unit: its own name, or `name@k` when another binder shares
  the name or the name is a prelude word (so the word stays a free variable for the
  interpreter, L1.18); temporaries are `$k`. Because names are unique, a default is filled by
  let-binding the call's arguments under the parameters' names and then the defaults in their
  own dependency order, with no capture. Statements of a scope are ordered by their uses; a
  cycle, self-reference included, is an error that names layer L6 (recursion). `e else d` and
  `x?.f` match any atom (`AnyAtom`, `#_`), exact while atoms are loose (before L2). A call
  through a value (lambda, prelude word) passes arguments by position only; named arguments
  there wait for types (L1.26). `test` tables belong to top-level functions; a key is the
  argument, or the tuple of all arguments in declaration order. Deferred as errors: rationals,
  integers beyond 64 bits and named atom payloads (L1.17), `is` (L1.22), lambda defaults
  (L1.26), overloads and fields (L2).
  Self-reference resolves to itself; cycles are a later step's.
- L1.17: the spec said both IEEE `==` (sec:numeric) and "`==` reflexive at f64, bitwise"
  (M0.11); IEEE wins for the language's `==` and the comparisons, and bitwise identity stays
