  for keys and law checks (Rust `Eq` on `Value`). M0.11 and the axioms are corrected. `==`
  compares atoms instead of passing them through, since it has an arrow at every type; the
  m0.10 helpers that used `x == x` to pass a failure now match `#_`. Comparing numbers of two
  kinds, or `1r + 1`, is a type error; `1 == 1.0` is false. A payload is all by position or
  all by name; field order is not part of the value. `==` on functions is a type error naming
  L1.22. The size limit of 2^20 bits, and the elaboration error of a rat literal past it, were
  removed by CAP-bits: numbers have no size limit.
- L1.18: several failing operands now merge into `#errs` of the set of them, replacing M0.9's
  "leftmost atom wins" (DESIGN.md M0.9 (a) is corrected), so `#a + #b` and `#b + #a` agree.
  The set is a family mapping each failure to itself. Origin sets wait for origin tokens
  (L1.20). Cast grammars: `int` reads `-?digits`; `rat` reads `3r`, `-1r/3r`, `2/4` or
  `0.25`; `f64` reads `-?` then `inf`, `nan` or `digits[.digits][e-?digits]`, and a finite
  text past the float range is `#inexact`. `+5`, `.5` and `infinity` are `#invalid`.
  `upper` uses full case mapping (`"straße"` to `"STRASSE"`). Fixed notation up to 10^21
  means `1e16` prints as `10000000000000000.0`.
- L1.19: read failures are the bare `#ioError` the types write (`Files = Path ->? String |
  #ioError`, `LineNo -> String | #ioError`), since payload patterns arrive at L2; only the
  write acknowledgment carries `#ioError(why)`, and the program never sees it. A port pushed
  twice is caught at run time, not statically, because two push sites on two branches are
  legal. Elaboration turns `v[k]` into `tupleGet` for a literal key and an application
  otherwise, so until L1.22 the evaluator reads a family or a lookup port either way.
  Files are written through `Lines` ports bound to paths (`out=path`); a `Files` output
  waits for families (L3).
- L1.20: a non-recursive call is named by its call site in the static half of the address
  (the spec's list of region instances leaves calls out); recursion is keyed by entry key
  at L6. A region's operator calls are aimed at the operand's position, so two operators of
  one region differ though their keypaths match. A primitive a region calls is no fact.
  Recording is opt-in and does not change the value; retention policy arrives with
  sec:security's lineage sampling.
- L1.21: the spec left the program test form to this step; it is a table on `main` (a
  `Files` port given as a lambda, since a family display is L3) and the `// ✗` marks, the
  convention the documentation's fences already use. Only the first rejecting phase reports,
  so marks in a later phase go unchecked; an aborting wave fails its row. The spec's command
  is `zed test`; the binary is `zedc`, as for `run`.
- L1.22: the tuple and morphism parts are not reduced to a canonical form, so equal sets can
  print differently; type equality is subtyping both ways (L1.23). `is` can tell a function
  from other values and nothing finer until families carry their types (L3). A keypath is a
  tuple, a family a morphism; `() -> V` is keyed by the empty tuple; `->?` is partial at
  each level. `Lines` and `Files` are port types only.
- L1.23: a clause of morphism types is decided one negated type at a time, so it is sound
  and incomplete when several negated types together cover the positive part. A value type
  with a parameter under a complement or right of `\` counts as possibly empty (monotonicity).
  All members of a group take the same parameters. Tuple components that mention the group
  stay lazy nodes, so building a recursive type never unfolds it. The memo is per thread and
  never cleared. `Env` and its members form a reference cycle that leaks, once per instance.
  Recursive types are kernel `Group`s here; `type` declarations map onto them at L2.
- L1.24: the spec's "symbolic affine bounds" are given a numeric floor, since the join of
  `[0]` and `[1..=n]` is `[0..=max(0, n)]` and not `[0..=n]`; without it every counting loop
  loses its bound. Ends stay 64-bit and saturate outward. A symbol is named by its source text
  and its range is not known to the domain (so `a::len >= 0` is not used); the reduction with
  the other domains, which would supply it, is L1.39. Bounds with different terms are not
  compared, so `int[0..=n]` and `int[0..=n + m]` are both kept by a meet. The finite-set domain
  holds kernel values; strings' lengths are counted in characters (code points after NFC).
- L1.25: a preimage is a box; a relation among inputs is projected and marked necessary until
  L1.38. `x * x <= 99` reaches `[-9..=9]` through the power reading, exact, rather than by the
  enumeration the plan names. Because `==` and `!=` compare failures as data, a comparison whose
  operands may fail keeps its box unless the other side is a constant integer; the kernel's own
  `&&` and `||` pass failures through, so their union is exact only when neither side can fail
  (the surface `&&` elaborates to a branch). There is no hash primitive in the kernel, so the
  stops are string operations, foreign names and terms outside the scalar catalog. Congruences
  for `mod` over unbounded operands are L1.39.
- L1.26: an undeclared parameter is a class of a union-find forest (`check::Class`): the
  carriers its values may take, and whether other values. Uses narrow and merge classes; the
  body is checked once; a class left with several carriers gives a bounded variable (`area(w,
  h) = w * h` is `(w: T, h: T) -> T for T in int | f64 | rat`), and one holding a literal the
  first of int, f64 and rat, since a body elaborates once, so `f(x) = x * 2` is `(x: int) ->
  int`. A nested function reads the outer classes, so its signature may name the outer
  function's variable. A use that would empty a class is the error, at that use. No derived
  variables: an operator's result over a class is the class and the atoms of every arrow
  still possible. A float literal never adopts int. A type variable used as a tuple or a function
  gives `_` (`first(p) = p[0]` is `(p: T) -> _`). Atoms pass through operators but not into
  declared functions or lambdas. A divisor whose type holds no zero adds no `#divByZero`. The
  first arrow that fits a combination gives its result. An application over unions keeps one
  state per set of arrows still alive, with no cap.
- L1.27: L1 has no `type` declaration, so every atom an L1 program meets is loose, and
  run-time `else` defaults every atom; step L2.5 must set the family marking from `type`, and
  step L2.4 make elaboration keep the atoms the checker protected (an arm before `#_`). A pattern with a
  payload keeps its atom in the type.
- L1.28: a local binding counts as demanded when its body names it, even in a branch cut
  later. `Program::stage` is not yet used by the `zed` command; the residual program is used
  by lowering (L1.33). The `left` entries of `stage::Report` carry the term and the stop;
  L1.29 turns them into derivations (DESIGN.md sec:diagnostics now requires every rejection
  to show the full derivation: each constraint, its site, and the path it took).
- L1.29: no ✗ variant in `zed/fences/variants` reaches its documented error at L1: four
  stop at an L2 declaration, three fail to parse at L1, two use `…`, and the two that pass
  the mark check pass for other reasons (`Point` undefined; a parse error on the marked line). The test pins what
  each gives; the step that enables a variant must change its pin to the documented error.
  The staging report (`stage::Report.left`) is not a rejection and gives each left term its
  reason without a derivation. A first exclusion of "stale" search records cut the chain of
  a top-level value read in a searched body; it was removed, since every run re-records each
  subexpression before deriving any error in it. A step of a block derives no further than
  the block (its export is not followed yet), and a literal binding shows as "this
  expression is 4" rather than as a literal. A derivation is not cut: each expression in
  it is derived once (docs/thresholds.md, replacement 2), so it is at most the size of the
  program.
- L1.30: keys are syntactic (the `Debug` print of kernel terms), so an alpha-renaming or a
  renumbered `name@k` binder reruns a row that a canonical key would reuse. Kernel names are
  numbered per source name in binder order, so adding a same-named binder before another
  renames it and reruns the rows that reach it. Results are never evicted. `zedc run`
  compiles its one unit once, so it calls `run::compile` directly. Found on the way:
  `zedc test` printed a rejection's message without its derivation; fixed.
- L1.31 (DESIGN.md L1.31): regions are isolated; a value from outside enters as a capture,
  an input after the node's fixed ones received as a trailing argument. State edges are linear
  and enter only match and port regions. A certificate region holds only kernel nodes. The
  validator checks structure only; edge-type fit is the checker's, for the term lowered from.
  The text form starts `zir 1`, one line per header, node and results, keyed by path, read
  back in any order; a literal pattern is `=v`; a recursive type has no text form until L2.
  The binary form starts `ZIR\0` and the version, stores edges as their text, floats by their
  bits. Errors give line and column, or byte offset.
- L1.32 (DESIGN.md L1.32): on ZIR the binder rule renames match-pattern binders to their
  index in the pattern, the written name a hint on the argument edge; the other rules run per
  value edge in preorder. The address hashes `zed-zir\0`, the M0.7 version and the binary
  form of the normal form (a level name begins with NUL, which the text form cannot read).
- The two zeros (DESIGN sec:type_resolution, "Equality and order corner rules"): `==` and the orders read `0.0 == -0.0`, and a tuple compares component by component in IEEE order (`Value::surface_cmp`); key formation and lookup turn `-0.0` into `0.0` at any depth (`Value::key`), which the kernel had not done before. Identity keeps them apart: kernel `Eq` on values, laws, and the content address, which hashes a float by its bits and every NaN as `0x7ff8000000000000`. Tests: `m0_11_prelude_axioms_and_limits/signed_zero.rs` and the kernel test `the_two_zeros_are_one_key`.
- L1.33 (DESIGN.md L1.33): a primitive used as a value lowers to a `prim` node with no
  inputs, not to a lambda, since the kernel tells them apart; a variadic primitive has no such
  form. Captures and state edges are taken on demand; a function that uses a port is inlined
  at each call. CSE keys a node by the address of a one-node region; a node with a state input
  never repeats one, so effects are never merged. Edges carry `_` until code generation.
- L1.34 (DESIGN.md L1.34): a constant is static and never counted; integers, floats, booleans,
  the empty tuple and bare atoms are unboxed. A call to a callee the plan cannot see passes
  owned. Loop and family regions run in the kernel, which borrows their inputs; a family's
  entries are kernel data until L3.17. Scalar, family and port nodes take their value from the
  kernel, which runs each node alone; tuples, closures, calls, branches and matches run in the
  checking machine, so it keeps the program's sharing.
- L1.35 (DESIGN.md L1.35): MLIR is reached through its text form and `mlir-opt`/`mlir-translate`,
  so no C++ build enters the tree; the C API is adopted only if compile time measured at L28
  shows the process boundary matters. MLIR's bufferization and ownership-based deallocation
  are not used for Zed values; the L1.34 plan is applied before the lowering.
- T3 (Z8, Z9, Z9b): a node holds its region and index, not a copy, and paths exist only in
  messages. The `if` releases an owned condition the arms do not take at the test, on the
  path where it is not a boolean, which saves an `rt_dec` per `if`; counts equal the
  machine's. `docs/wip/z8-z9-stopped.patch` stays, for the author to delete; this
  `early` release was the one part of it not yet landed. One function's frame is linear
  in its words, so a term thousands deep needs a stack of megabytes at run time. Sharing
  slots between sibling arms would reduce that.
- L1.36 (DESIGN.md L1.36): a value is a word of a tag and a payload, returned in two
  registers. Arms are inline; a lambda body is a function, with an entry function taking every
  argument owned for calls the plan cannot resolve. Nodes outside the inline tier are run by
  the runtime through the kernel, so the native and machine counts agree one for one; the
  runtime's state is thread-local, and a hook never calls generated code. A match that native
  pattern matching does not decide is decided by the kernel, and the two must agree. The
  object file is checked for reproducibility and its symbols; linking it into an executable
  comes with `zed build`. The workspace's `unsafe_code` lint is relaxed only in `zed-native`.
- L1.37 (DESIGN.md L1.37): the checker records each top-level `=` binding's type, and
  `elab::Def::first_order` says whether it has no function component. A binding typed `_`, as
  a higher-order call's result is at L1, is compared too, since both engines refuse a function
  result with the same error. Outcomes are compared as text: the printed value or the error,
  and for a wave each port's bytes. A `Files` port in the harness holds the input text at every
  path. `oracle::ENABLED` is the enabled layer and rises with each layer; the corpus joins at
  L3. The reorder-facet rule (a float compared by its type and keys) is due at L9.
- L1.38 (DESIGN.md L1.38): since T4 the solver's integers are unbounded (`store::int`, i128 while a value fits), so no coefficient refuses a question.
  Replacement 4 removed `VARIABLES`, `ATOMS`, `INTERMEDIATE` and `KEPT_CONJUNCTIONS`: the
  Omega test eliminates a variable only while the rows stay within the input's count, decides
  an inexact step by the real and dark shadows or leaves it undecided (`Entailment::Undecided`),
  and does not split a formula into its conjunctions: disjuncts are pruned against the base,
  a point is sought greedily with one level of lookahead, and a projection meets the unions of
  each disjunct's projections; a scope projects a part of its relation again only when its
  names' intervals or congruences changed (docs/thresholds.md §4, COMPLEXITY.md S16); a product atom's countermodel is checked against the real product, and a
  point where it fails is "not proved". `Preimage::relation` is kept only on a result that is
  not exact. The congruence `x mod 7 != 3` over a range is kept as a relation, necessary
  (enumeration of ranges was removed by docs/thresholds.md, replacement 8).
- L1.38, T4 (cross-check XV Q9): `presburger::decide` answers whether a conjunction has an integer
  point without building one. A system is split into parts that share no variable; a part of
  two variables is decided exactly (`twovar`), one whose rows are all of two unit terms by the
  zero-weight cycle parity test (`utvpi`), and the rest by the Omega test with dark and gray
  shadows. Splinters are searched one level deep: a point that could lie only in a splinter of
  a splinter is refused (`Refusal::Shadows`), a judgment taken here; the elimination that would
  enlarge the rows is refused (`Refusal::Growth`) as DESIGN L1.38 records. The simplex is only a
  proof that a system has no rational point, and gives the last step a vertex to try. Dantzig's
  rule and Bland's rule decide the same feasibility, but the vertex differs, so the rule can
  change a Growth refusal into a point: with Bland's rule from the first pivot, a unit
  three-term family at 16 variables went from a point to Growth. The checker asks, for each
  function other than `main` whose body the linear-relation domain states, whether the body can
  hold and whether it can fail (`run::linear_questions`); a refusal is a rejection with its
  cause, the function's span and the constraints, and a decided question rejects nothing. The
  parameters' types are not read there, so the domains are unbounded, and a question typed
  bounds would decide can be refused. Of 400 dense four-variable systems with coefficients up
  to 7, about 150 are refused with Growth. A refused dark shadow is the answer: its splinters
  are not searched, since they could only turn the refusal into a point, and searching them
  ran the test again once per lower bound of each dark elimination (a chain into a refused
  block, 64 to 512 variables: 33.5 times the work before, about 11 after; the oracle's 400
  four-variable answers did not change). The simplex switches to Bland's rule for good after 4
  to 12 pivots on the `tight` probe family, so in practice it is Bland's rule; a rule that
  returns to Dantzig's at each new least total violation was tried and measured worse (shifted
  sparse family 26.9 times the work from 128 to 1,024, over the limit of 24; `tight` at 64
  variables 46 s against 9 s), and was not kept.
- L1.38, T4 repair 4: `utvpi` finds integer potentials by Bellman–Ford–Moore with Tarjan's
  subtree disassembly instead of a rational simplex, and needs no rationals (random unit rows,
  1,024 variables and 4,096 rows: 75 s to 0.06 s). The splinter search takes, at each dark
  elimination, the side with fewer bounds (Pugh's lemma holds for `-x` too), and the first
  refused splinter is the answer, so a later splinter that has a point is not reached: a
  judgment taken here for linear work, which left the oracle's 400 answers unchanged. Pugh's
  example inside a chain is refused with Shadows, though it has no integer point. The general
  simplex on the `tight` family is unchanged and goes to cross-review xt; its test is
  `l01_38_presburger::tight`, ignored. A fill rule measured for xt is in COMPLEXITY.md, S18.
- L1.38, XT-R (design R of the xt round-2 synthesis): the simplex is gone, and with it
  `Q::mul`, `lt` and `abs`; `simplex.rs` keeps only `Q` and `Term`. A system the Omega test
  cannot eliminate goes to box stages: each variable's bounds are tightened once per row from
  the original bounds, an empty box shows the rows empty, and the point of the box nearest
  zero, checked against every row, is a model; otherwise the answer is Growth. Before the
  Omega test, `quotient` merges proportional columns by a unimodular substitution and lifts a
  model back by the Bézout factors. `utvpi` finds potentials in topological order when the
  unit rows form a DAG, O(V+E), and by Bellman–Ford–Moore otherwise. Since UTVPI-GOR the
  cyclic case is Goldberg and Radzik's search (cyclic hub 7.9 times the work from n to 8n,
  was 42.5); since UTVPI-NEAR it races Goldberg and Radzik's search, BF-Dijkstra and Duong's
  near-linear search, the first to finish deciding (BAD-GOR augmented 7.8 times from k to
  8k, Goldberg and Radzik alone 59.8; COMPLEXITY.md C24). Open: BAD-GOR
  joined with a fan still grows 60.6 times (`bad_gor_and_a_fan_scale`, ignored; C24;
  item UTVPI-union); main before it grows 56.2 times there.
  Decisions lost, option A: the linear program decided questions that design R refuses. The
  oracle's 400 four-variable systems with coefficients up to 7 go from 229 sat, 21 unsat,
  150 Growth to 213, 3, 184 (BOX-5pt, below, recovers the systems with a point: 255, 3,
  142); in `random.rs` one projection of the 60, of `y` on a term with
  `div`, `mod`, `min` and `if`, is [-4..=5] where the points take [0..=5], since a question
  it asks of a disjunct is refused. The two asserts that counted these
  (`oracle.rs`, small coefficients; `random.rs`, projections within the hull) were changed to
  the new counts, with the reason in their comments: they measured the LP's strength, not
  correctness, and every answer given is still checked against enumeration. Other refusals
  of systems that have a point, each asserted: the checker's question in `checker.rs`
  (the rows hold at (4,0,1,0)); Codex's regular 8-variable system, refused under all 40,320
  namings, so the same answer; x0 fixed at 1 to 6 with the others in [-2,2]
  (`stages.rs`); three variables in many rows tight at (3,-2,5). Answers do not depend on
  names or row order on 48,000 reorderings. The 12,000 systems tight at a corner (seed
  0x38_3b) give no wrong answer: 2,550 sat, 9,282 unsat, 168 Growth. Negative controls, run
  once and not committed: without the DAG path the acyclic hub fails (52.6 times the work);
  without the quotient 3 tests fail; with the box test reverted to Growth 4 tests fail and
  the corner oracle's refusals go from 168 to 5,074, which no wrong answer showed, so its
  counts are now asserted. Flaw named, not fixed here: `Scope.refusals` in `product.rs` is
  written (`:1011`, `:1239`, `:1247`) and joined (`:864`) but never read (no reader in `crates`
  or `tests`); the xe round 3 item owns it. Fixed since by TD-refusals (c3d19a6):
  `Scope::warnings` reports each refusal as a warning.
- L1.38, BOX-5pt (XT3 report, B3): the box stage tries five points of the box of the bounds
  in turn, the point nearest zero, the lower corner, the upper corner, and the midpoints
  rounded down and up, each taken variable by variable (a missing bound gives that variable
  its value nearest zero), and the first that holds every row is the model; none is Growth.
  Five passes over the terms; no name, row order or size enters. The decisions with a point
  that only the linear program made are recovered and more: the oracle's 400 four-variable
  systems go from 213 sat, 3 unsat, 184 Growth to 255, 3, 142 (229 sat with the linear
  program); the 12,000 systems tight at a corner from 2,550, 9,282, 168 to 2,586, 9,282, 132;
  Codex's regular system is Ok(true) under all 40,320 namings (was Growth under each); x0
  fixed at 4 in `stages.rs` is Ok(true). These four asserts changed, as the XT3 probe
  measured, and no other test. The 18 empty systems the linear program showed empty stay
  refused (cross-review round 3, XR3). New: a system whose only point in [-1, 1]^8 is the
  upper corner (the regular rows plus `v[i] + v[i + 1] - v[i + 2] >= 1` around the cycle;
  the regular rows alone hold at 38 points there, not one as the item's brief said), and the
  naming tests in `oracle.rs` now decide each system under all n! orders of its names (24
  for the two four-variable families; since repair 1 the two namings of the two-variable
  family run twice, so every system gets at least four row orders: 174,000 systems in
  all): no answer depends on the
  names, so the doubt in part E of the XT3 report is not borne out on these families.
  Scaling: three variables in many rows, 64 → 512 rows, 9.0 times the work (1.88M → 16.9M
  instructions, Growth). Negative controls, run once and not committed: with only the point
  nearest zero 5 L1.38 tests fail (the four above and the upper-corner test); with only the
  point nearest zero and the lower corner the upper-corner test fails; recomputing the bounds
  once per row in `box_point` makes the scaling test fail at 50.6 times the work.
  Repair 1: in that family every point misses within the first two rows, so a second
  scaling test,
  `a_box_point_held_by_many_rows_scales` (three variables in [-10, 10], 256 → 2,048 rows
  holding at the upper corner, Ok(true) after a pass over every row), measures 9.0 times the
  work (8.86M → 79.3M); a row check that re-reads rows 0..=j at row j fails it at 44.5.
- L1.38, HULL3 (XT3 report, B4): with three live variables, one of which has a unit
  coefficient in every row on one side, `store/hull3.rs` chooses the pairs of a lower and an
  upper bound on it whose combinations cut out the real projection on the other two (the
  edges where the top of the polyhedron meets its bottom, O(m) pairs in O(m log m)); the unit
  side makes the integer projection the real one, so `twovar` decides the rest exactly. The
  dispatch on three live variables is a structural class, as the orchestrator ruled, not a
  threshold. Tests: the shadow equals all pairs at every half-integer point of a window on 800
  random systems and on degenerate ones (planes through one line or one point, normals in one
  plane, unbounded, empty, parallel bounds that do not meet); a new oracle of 400 systems over
  three variables in [-6, 6]^3 with a unit side agrees with enumeration with no refusal;
  2·x2 = 2·x0 + 1 (no unit side) stays Ok(false). Changed asserts, each a count of how much is
  decided, all answers checked against enumeration: the oracle 255, 3, 142 → 261, 3, 136; the
  corner oracle 2,586, 9,282, 132 → 2,611, 9,306, 83; the random terms' projections wider than
  the hull of their values 1 → 0; `three_variables_in_many_rows_scale` Growth → Ok(true).
  Scaling: that test, 64 → 512 rows, 12.1 times the work (89.2M → 1,082M instructions);
  `hull3::the_shadow_scales`, 128 → 1,024, 13.1. Negative control, run once and not
  committed: all m² pairs fail the first at 68.2. Open: three live variables with no unit
  side, and four or more, stay refused (HULL3-GEN, after XR3).
- L1.38, Q1-ROWBOX and HUB-SCALE (cross-review XR3 Q1): the box test reads each wide row
  once and keeps, for each variable, the greatest lower and least upper rational bound the
  rows give it with their other terms at their largest; two that cross show the rows empty.
  Changed asserts, each a count of how much is decided and checked against enumeration:
  0x38_0a 261, 3, 136 → 261, 4, 135, and 0x38_3b 2,611, 9,306, 83 → 2,611, 9,320, 69. The
  brief expected 23 of the 64 at 0x38_3b; that is the count without `hull3` (with `hull3`
  switched off for one run: 2,586, 9,305, 109, 23 more than the 2,586, 9,282, 132 before
  `hull3`), and `hull3` already decides 9 of those 23. 0x38_41 (`refused > 20`) now runs 4,000 systems instead of
  3,000: the row test leaves 17 refusals at 3,000 and 25 at 4,000. Not shown empty: the
  rows of `stages::CROSSING` with each constant raised by 1 to 7 have no rational point, but
  no pair of rows crosses over the tightened box; they stay refused (Growth) since the
  resolvent stage was not adopted. Negative controls, run once and not committed: `<=` for
  `<` in the crossing checks fails 3 unit tests (the `i128` edge, the open variable, the
  systems with an integer point); `row_box_empty` run again over every row of each variable
  of a row fails both scaling tests (hub family 44.6 and 175.2, with a crossing 46.5 and
  209.5); the bounds rebuilt for each row from the rows before it fail the hub family (32.8
  and 49.6) but not the family with a crossing (12.4 and 21.3), where the crossing is found
  after about a third of the rows.
- L1.38, LIVE3 (author decision 2026-11-03: a linear program in a fixed dimension is allowed at three live variables only): `store::live3` decides a part left with three variables, once no elimination keeps the rows, completely, so Growth and Shadows now need four variables or more. Method: Megiddo's prune-and-search for a linear program in dimension three (deterministic, by medians, no simplex and no random choice), in exact rationals with infinitesimals for ties; a simplex of points of the rows from six such programs, rounded with a fixed ratio (the rows lie within the simplex scaled by 25 about its centroid); an exact LLL reduction of that shape, giving Babai's point (tried against the rows) or a direction whose width over the rows is at most w(3), a constant of dimension three and not a size; each value along it is a plane, given two variables by a unimodular completion and decided by `twovar::point`. Entered from the box stage of `Omega::run` where the box decides nothing and `live == 3`, and from the splinter search, so a gray shadow over three variables is decided too. The answer is order-free because the method is complete; the point found may depend on order. `Int` got 64-bit fast paths in `div_floor`, `mod_floor` and `*` (about 10% fewer instructions in callgrind). Counts changed, each with its reason in the test's comment: the small-coefficient oracle (0x38_0a) 261, 3, 136 → 274, 4, 122; the corner oracle (0x38_3b) 2,611, 9,306, 83 → 2,631, 9,331, 38 (both measured after the rebase onto main with HULL3); refused systems in the three-term naming test 13 (bound lowered from more than 20 to more than 10); the checker's, `omega.rs`'s and the `l01_21` row test's Shadows examples moved to four variables (the three-variable ones are now decided); `s22::chains_of_slow_families` counts 6 boxes found exact by classes of remainders, was 7, since `mod7 k=2`, refused by Shadows in the transfer before, is now decided there with the same exact box, and the Growth message says four variables or more. The disjunct-order test is split into four quarters of the same 2,000 formulas (each asserts more than 250 of 500 reordered, together at least the old more than 1,000), since live3 made the whole take 42 s under load 58 (control 20 s): measured alone at load 60, 16.9, 13.8, 10.8 and 18.4 s. New tests in `l01_38_presburger::live3`: random bounded and unbounded systems, flat systems and equalities, rationally empty systems, Reeve tetrahedra under unimodular maps, all against enumeration; corner cases; order independence; the Omega test on three variables. Scaling, n and 8n in instructions (repair 1, after the verifier found that the first rows family was decided by the first linear program, so LLL and the planes were never measured): Reeve's tetrahedron of height 7 shrunk, with m rows that each hold the whole body with slack 0 to 5, so every stage runs (22 planes); rows 256 → 2,048, 8.18 times (1.92e9 → 1.57e10); bits 64 → 512 at 8 rows, 12.81 times at power 1 (both sizes past the i128 path of `Int`); the same body under a shear of B/2-bit entries, 10.28 times (probe). Negative controls, run once and not committed: an all-pairs `Lin2::minus` pass in each round of `solve2` fails the rows test at 33.1; an extra factor of B in `Q::mul` fails the bits test on bytes at 35.0 (instructions 21.7); an all-pairs pass of additions over the rows of each plane is not caught (10.2), since it is small next to `twovar`. Rebased onto main (HULL3 and the presburger.rs change): the `hull3` stage runs first where a unit side exists and `live3` at the box stage after it; HULL3 decides nothing LIVE3 does not (the oracle counts are those of LIVE3 alone), so it is subsumed in what it decides (ledger item HULL3-subsumed); which runs first is for that item to measure. `more_random_guarded_chains_keep_their_preimages` counts 55 made exact, was 56: the transfer before, which shares the solver, now decides by `live3` the gray shadow of one chain (`(if ((i mod 3) == 2) { … } < 6)`), so both are exact and alike there (shown by turning off the call of `live3` in splinters, which gives 56 again).
- LR1: the failures of the last, failed expansion stay in `far` and `expected`. That pass reaches the operator a flat `*` loop stops at, so it records what the loop records; dropping them would lose operators from the messages. docs/plan_dag.md's wording says the opposite and its aim, unchanged messages, is met: with `Additive` and `Multiplicative` made left-recursive in a trial copy, `errors.rs` passed, and 3 of the 323 bracketing cases differed, each a flat chain now nested to the left. On that trial copy a parse took the same time per token as the flat rules (5.2 µs). The real grammar with no left recursion is no slower than before LR1 (3.8 to 5.1 µs per token against 4.1 to 5.2 µs, under load).
- LR1, for LR2: as in the paper, `E = E '+' E | N` gives a right-associative tree, so a rung must use the form `E = E '+' T | T`. A loop written in a left-recursive rule before a token that fails is walked again at each growth, so such a prefix must be a rule of its own (COMPLEXITY.md F35). A left-recursive cycle of two or more rules under a long list makes the same quadratic walk at each growth at the list's own token, so each rung must be directly left-recursive. The first alternative that matches ends a growth: with `Sum = Attempt | Sum '+' INT` and `Attempt = Sum 'q'`, a match of `Attempt` is kept on the next growth, so `Sum '+' INT` is not tried after it (a case in `leftrec.txt`).
- LR1: an entry that reads no seed is no longer matched again, which changes one thing: a rule first matched inside `!` keeps no failures, so a later read of its entry records none, as in a parser with no left recursion. As first built, a growth at that token re-recorded them. On random grammars 0.06% of error reports lost expected items or moved `far` back, never forward, and trees did not change; with no `!` in the grammars, 6.24 million comparisons showed no difference. The real grammar parses in the same time as before (4.4 to 5.9 µs per token against 4.4 to 5.7 µs, alternating runs under load).
- LR2: a marked alternative makes no node, so a `~R` whose layer is above the lowest layer of `R` reports the layer of `R`. `Grammar::read` refuses it after all rules are read, and `LitType = SignedLit L10` carries the layer that `~SignedLit L10` lost. `an_alternative_past_its_ring_is_not_tried_through_a_mark` now gives its rule `T` the layer L5, since its old grammar is refused.
- LR2: skip marks. The plan's motive, fixed parentheses and punctuation, does not hold: a literal already makes no node in either parser. The noise was the ladder of about 14 one-child nodes per expression. Options scored: `~R` on a rule name at the top of an alternative, 9, one written mark, one reader rule, a node dropped exactly where the mark stands; a `skip(e)` that drops a child, 2, nothing to drop; splicing a node's children into its parent, 3, the ladder node stays; collapsing every alternative of a single reference, 4, implicit, and it would change which forms name a layer. `~R` was applied.
- LR2: `Update = Name ('.' Name)* '=' Expr L2 | KeyPath '=' Expr L5` keeps its first alternative unchanged, because a `Path` there would lose the label role of its names. `Annotated` moved before `UnitAnnot`.
- LR2: `Expr` and `Expr12` still give one node each over a lone operand, since they are no plain pass (`Expr12 = Or ElseTail*`). The fuzz of `tools/grammar` reports `('Annotated', 2)` as never exercised; `cases.txt` covers that line by a new case.
- LR2: the tests leak the syntax tree of a chain of a million operators, because `Drop` of a deep `Expr` still recurses. T2-parse removes that recursion. The depth a parse reaches changes a little with the grammar: at a 2 MiB stack, nested calls overflow at 163 levels against 171 before, parentheses at 206 against 183.
- LR4: `Expr12`'s lone-operand alternative is now `~Or`, so a lone operand leaves no `Expr12` node (`a_name_leaves_no_node_on_the_rungs_of_the_ladder` now expects `["Expr", "Name"]`); the LR2 note above about `Expr12` giving a node no longer holds. The joins Planning bullet of DESIGN sec:joins said mixed join kinds need parentheses, which the grammar never required; it now says that `&&` and `\|\|` do not mix and a chain of `&&` kinds joins from the left, as `-` subtracts.
- LR4: the tests are in `l01_08_parser/lr4_assoc.rs`, not a folder `tests/steps/lr4_assoc/`, because a step folder's name must be a step (`is_step_name`). The fuzz reports `('JoinExpr', 1)` as never exercised, in addition to `('Annotated', 2)` and `('JoinExpr', 0)` before: `JoinExpr` is read only inside a lookahead.
- L1.39 (DESIGN.md L1.39): reductions until a round changes nothing; a type widens to its
  whole base type; only a name whose finite set lists its integers is enumerated, and a
  constraint whose form does not show it ends removes nothing (docs/thresholds.md 8); enumerations remembered per constraint and static values; a
  false static constraint gives bottom; a test's linear relation is kept only when it needs no
  auxiliary variable; the walk to the arms skips tests, function bodies and patterns;
  `zed_kernel::on_stack` is a no-op until T2-mid deletes it. The
  `isPrime` example waits for L3. The preimage transfer remembers each (term, scope) result.
  Replacement 5 removed `CALL_DEPTH` and `TRANSFER_FUEL`: a call of a lambda whose call is
  being read stops (`StopKind::Recursion`), so calls nest no deeper than the number of
  lambdas; a call whose input-reading arguments are integers is summarized once per lambda,
  scope, landmark-snapped argument intervals and snapped demand, and its preimage is the
  summary's box met with each argument's preimage (docs/thresholds.md §5). A summary keeps
  only the intervals it narrowed its inputs to: one first computed inside another call kept
  that call's parameter box and cut points elsewhere, while claiming exact. The linear-relation
  translation remembers each call by lambda, scope and argument forms (COMPLEXITY.md S15).
- L1.39a (DESIGN.md L1.39a): the checker has no int ranges at L1 (`int[..]` and `i32` are L10), so the domain of each operand is decided as `**`'s exponent is, from its listed values and what its text shows (`Ck::known`: literals, names bound by `name = e`, `+ - * div mod **`, the bit words, constants folded by the kernel); an int from input, or one only a test narrows (`if n >= 0 { popCount(n) }`), is rejected (COMPLEXITY.md C26). `exponent` now reads names through `Ck::known` too, so `k = 2 + 1; 2 ** k` is accepted. `w: i32 = shl(1, 30)` and `x.bitAnd(255)` with `x : int[0..]` are tested on `store::interval::bit_word`, the transfer the store's product applies, since L1 has no `i32` annotation. An int literal at `f64Bits`'s `f64` parameter is the `f64` it names. Two failures combine as under every strict word (`rotl(#a, #b, width: 8)` is `#a + #b`'s value). No axioms of the bit words were added to the prelude. A binder's value is read by `Ck::known` only when a bit word or an exponent reads its name, so an unread constant such as `x = 3 ** 30000000 + 5 ** 30000000;` is never folded, and `Ck::known` reads children before parents on an explicit stack: `l01_39a_bit_words::deep` reads `popCount` through a chain of 20,000 bindings on an 8 MiB stack (a recursive `Ck::known` overflows it). The checker's scaling tests run on a 512 MiB stack, since the parser and the checker recurse once per nested call.
- L1.39b (DESIGN.md L1.39b): a derivation is still built when its error is made, not when it is printed, since every error the checker keeps is reported (`run::placed` prints each one's steps), but for the first run's errors that a replay of a recursive need drops, whose derivations are still built; what changed is that nothing a derivation reads is put into words before then, and that a repair trial, whose errors are only counted, builds none. A type shown through `Ck::show` in an origin keeps the carriers of its classes at the moment it was recorded (`Masks`), so the printed text is that of before. `exact_steps` stays 0 for every unit because no checker code calls the store yet (wt/xv/SYNTHESIS.md); the exact test builds `Scope`s directly. A program of 2,400 functions each calling the one before overflows an 8 MiB stack in `run::load` (found while writing the scaling test, not caused by this step; the test calls the first function from each instead). The 300-function scaling test does not see a step that grows with the origins recorded before it (L1.39b verify#1 control 2 passed it), as each function costs the checker about 1.7 million instructions; `checking_functions_of_many_parameters_scales` (L1.39b-origin-scale) measures the check alone over 100 and 800 functions of 40 parameters, about 10,000 instructions per origin, and that control fails it at 51.3x. A binding `y = x * 2` or `y = y0 + 1` costs the checker about 1.4 million instructions and 210 KB, linear in their number but large; not looked into here.
- L1.39c (DESIGN.md L1.39c): DESIGN keys `utf8`'s family from 1, while the array display `[a, b]` is keyed from 0 (sec:grammar ArrayExpr), so `fromUtf8` reads the values in key order and accepts both; it ignores the keys otherwise, so `{3 -> 105, -2 -> 104}` gives "hi" (decided here, not in DESIGN; DESIGN's own test `fromUtf8([192, 128])` uses a family keyed from 0). At L1 the surface has no family or array display (layer L3), so the `fromUtf8` tests of hand-built byte lists run on kernel terms. The checker types a family as `(int ->? int)` with no int range (L10), so at L1 no surface form reads a byte of `utf8` into a bit word (`utf8(s)[1]` is refused: `[1]` takes a tuple). Only a written call of `codePoint` gets the interval `0 ..= 1114111` in `Ck::known` (C28); the reduced product's transfer (`store::product`) gives `codePoint` no interval. A non-int value in `fromUtf8`'s family is a type error, and an int outside `0 ..= 255` is `#badUtf8`.
- Checker performance (L1.38): the elaboration retried each carrier combination with full
  derivations, so a sum of five inputs took 23.7 s; the trial runs are now quiet and only a
  shown failure is re-run with its derivation (3.7 s; the step tests 55 s → 45 s; snapshots
  unchanged).
- Known limits of the M0.5 store: the order and the stabilization recurse as deep as the graph
  (revisit at L1.24, where real programs give large graphs). Interval ends were 64-bit and
  rounded outward past that range; since TD-ends they are exact at any size (`End::Fin(Num)`,
  docs/thresholds.md replacement 10).
- OSP-order: the key of DESIGN.md M0.5 (name, content address, canonical form) is not total.
  Two fact cells of one static node at different keypaths have the same name, address and
  form, so only their source position or numbering would order them. `wto::ranks` refuses
  such a pair with `SameKey` rather than fall back on position; the nearest fix is the keypath
  as a fourth part of the key (a DESIGN decision, not made here). The checker (`check/mod.rs`)
  does not call `store::iterate` yet, so no checked program reaches the refusal; `wto` and
  `iterate` are the only places that compute an order of iteration.
- The kernel `fold` term carries the depth of the folded family (M0.10). The evaluator cannot
  read the depth off a family with no leaves, so without it `fold` of an empty two-level
  family gave `#empty` where its type gives the empty family, and laws 4 and 8 failed on the
  empty input. Typing supplies the depth; a leaf at another depth is the mixed-depth error.
- Kernel values are normalized: a key over an empty level is dropped, so the nonempty-level
  conditions of laws 1, 2 and 7 have no counterexample among values. They constrain the
  guaranteed domains, and are checked where `card` facts are (L1.24, L3.6).
- Fuel and nesting depth are limits of the reference evaluator and not a meaning (spec
  reference semantics (i), commit after a1ed53b); numbers have no size limit since CAP-bits. Law 11
  deletes a dead binding whose computation would pass a limit; that is not a counterexample,
  and the laws are checked on instances the evaluator can run.
- The harness compares outcomes as values (floats bitwise after NaN canonicalization) or as
  errors of one kind, since two sides that both collide may name different keypaths.
- Joins and conjunctions in the reduced product (ST, repair 4). A join replaces the conjuncts
  of a changed block in place (`Conjuncts::swap`, `Blocks::replace`): removing them first cut
  a linked chain's tree and built the whole component's blocks again, so joins one after
  another were quadratic. A conjunct of no names is decided when it joins the relation, and a
  false relation empties the scope; `!(t >= 0)` is stored as `-1 - t >= 0`; and a conjunction
  at the root whose whole has no linear relation is narrowed side by side. With these,
  `assume(a && b)` on names apart is never wider than `a` then `b`, and is sometimes
  narrower, which is sound. The tree sizes in `Blocks` are not reduced as conjuncts leave;
  they only choose which tree is rerooted, so balance and not results depends on them.
- Complementary disjuncts (ST, repair 5). `Formula::or` returns `true` for a disjunction that
  holds two complements, `g` and `!g` or `t >= 0` and `s >= 0` with `t + s` constant and at
  least -1, decided by a hash lookup of the negated term, not by the solver. A conjunct
  removed from a block of names whose other conjuncts still link its names is handled by
  placing those conjuncts again (`Blocks::split`); the union-find of trees cannot split, so
  any removal that would split a tree still rebuilds the component.

- L2.1: a display in body position (a lambda, `else` or arm body) without parentheses gets the parser's general "expected …" message, as the grammar's `(Block | !'{' Expr)` gives; the spec calls it a parse error whose repair is parentheses, and a message naming that repair is open. Two paths of one `with` clause where one is a prefix of the other (`c = …, c.x = …`) are accepted: the spec rejects only identical paths and does not state this case. The formatter prints `(p with { x = 1 }).x` with parentheses, since `.x` after the braces belongs to the `with`. A struct name used as a value or type before L2.4 is reported as not defined. The tests `l01_10`, `l01_11` and `l01_14` changed: a parenthesized display and a tuple parameter now build L2 forms, and the fences accept the refusal " is compiled from step L2.…". Four pins of `l01_29_diagnostics::variants` moved: those programs now parse past their struct or `type` line and stop at `-> int` (L10) or `match` (L2.2).
- L2.2: an empty `match` (`match x {}`) is a syntax error, since `Match` reads `nest(Arm % ',' ','?)`, which needs one arm; the spec does not say whether an empty match over an empty type is allowed. Two equal annotations on one declaration (`@memoized @memoized f = …`) are not rejected, and annotation arguments are not resolved, since every annotation is refused for now; the layer that builds each must decide both. `...` in annotation arguments is refused. A refused pattern binds nothing, so a use of its name in the arm body also gives "`n` is not defined", as at L2.1. The formatter writes every `match` one arm a line, even a short one. The corpus test of `l01_13_formatter::fences` asserts the exact layout of every program that builds except `10-regex-matcher.zed` (layer L19), named in the test: it builds past its import now, and its `hhmm` record display is wrapped by hand, so the corpus or the formatter's line wrapping must change when L19 resolves. The tests `l01_13_formatter::layout` (two new cases), `l01_29_diagnostics::variants` (the payload-by-position program now stops at its `type` line, L2.5) and the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding` changed.
- LR4-fix: LR4 was merged after a rebase onto L2.1 without a test run; at its own base no display read `Expr12`'s node, so its tests passed. Any builder that tells kids apart by rule name over a position where `Expr12` (or another `~` rule) stands has the same hazard; `display` was the only one found (the other rule-name tests in `ast` read `Update`, `PlainArg`, `Item`, `Params`, `ArrowParams`, `Type` and `FieldPat`, none of which a pass alternative yields). Negative control: the old rule-name test put back fails 7 tests of `l02_01_parser_structs`, the 3 reported and 4 of the 5 new ones (the fifth checks the refusal of a field given twice, which both pass).
- L3.1: `not (a in b)` is a syntax error, since Zed negates with `!` (`!(a in b)`); `a in b in c` is a syntax error, since `InTest` does not chain. `(..<)` is a syntax error: a range operator is not a `BinOp`. Nested parentheses around a section build one section, `((+))` is `(+)`. `[int]` in an expression is an array display of the name `int`, and a type only in type position. `{ x }` is a set and `{}` the empty map, as Display precedes Block in Primary. A `for` body must export: `for x in m { g(x); }` stops at its expression statement (L6). The formatter writes a `for` in parentheses as the operand of an operator and as a `for` source or guard, which the grammar does not need; `if` and `match` operands are still written bare (`if a { 1 } else { 2 } + 1`), as before. With the forms of L3 building, ten more corpus programs build and format to a fixed point, and each differs from its text only by hand layout the formatter does not do (`=` and trailing comments aligned, long lines wrapped, a `match` on one line in `23-join-zoo.zed`), listed in `l01_13_formatter::fences`; the corpus or the formatter must change for the test to compare them. Tests changed: `l01_08_parser::deep` (a spread suffix, L6, in place of a display at the bottom of a deep nest), `l01_09_postfix_chains::calls` (`unordered`, L9, in place of `::len`), `l01_11_statements::blocks` (`({ x })` is now a set), `l01_14_name_resolution::fences` (accepts " is compiled from step L3." and later-layer annotations, since fences using L3 forms now build), `l02_01_parser_structs::blocks` (sets and maps build), the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding`.
- L6.1: The grammar has no labeled `break`: a loop's name names its step index, and `break` ends the innermost loop, so `break i` in an inner loop is a break with the outer step as its value. `p'.x = 1;` and `p.x' = 1;` parse as method declarations (`T.m = e`); resolution refuses them as primed names (L6.3). The lexer takes one prime per name: `x''` is a lexical error. `x = 1 with;`, `x[] = 0;` and `break y -> y` are syntax errors. The statement order of a block is normalized in the builder as well as the formatter, so a text and its formatted text build to the same statements; the s-expression of a block lists `while` guards first and `until` guards last. A block that ends in an expression statement exports nothing, so `for x in m { g(x); }` and `x -> { y = x; y; }` now fail with "this block exports nothing" where they failed with "this form (ExprStmt) is built from layer L6". `a*.5` lexes `*.` as one token and is a syntax error ("expected IDENT"); `a * 0.5` is the product. With `loop` and indexed definitions building, nine more corpus programs build and format to a fixed point, each differing from its text only by hand layout, listed in `l01_13_formatter::fences`; three of them (15, 24, 28) write a `while` guard after the step, where DESIGN line 428 has the formatter write it first, so the corpus or that rule should change. Tests changed: `l01_08_parser::deep` (`unordered`, L9, in place of a spread suffix), `l01_09_postfix_chains::calls` (the spread case dropped), `l01_11_statements::blocks` (the exports-nothing error; a keyed push, L7, in place of a boundary), `l03_01_parser::later` (the exports-nothing error), `l01_13_formatter::fences` (the nine programs), `l01_14_name_resolution::fences` (accepts " is compiled from step L6."), the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding`.
- L25.8: The brief says "no other skipping" than the optimizer-equal mutants. A mutant can turn a program whose rows end into one that never ends: `w(positional f) = f(f); guard(positional x) = if x > 0 { w(w) } else { x } test { 0 -> 0 }` ends, and its mutant with `0` replaced by `-1` takes the `w(w)` branch, so `zedc test` on the mutated text did not end in 20 s (killed, exit 124). With no recursion at L1 the only source of such a loop is self-application, but a runner without a budget (docs/thresholds.md) cannot stop it. So a mutant's rows run only when `stage::ends` admits each closed term, and a mutant with a row it does not admit is counted "not run" and printed with its position. The cost: a higher-order call through a parameter (`twice(f: inc, x)`) is not admitted, so its definition's mutants are not run. The front end, the mixer and term equality recurse, so a chain of 800 additions needs the 8 MiB main stack (`zedc` runs on it; the scaling test uses `on_main_sized_stack`); `mutate`'s own walks use explicit stacks. `Info.quotients` records each `*` whose product the checker types with no `int` (`rat` or `f64`), where `/` is defined; `*` on `int` has no `/` mutant. Tests changed, since every step's `zed/` unit is run by the L1 harnesses and the new `fares.zed` adds 7 checks: `l01_33_lowering::corpus` (53 rows, 65 lowered terms), `l01_34_memory::corpus` and `l01_36_cranelift::corpus` (53 rows; 65 terms, 117 cells, 121 or 147 writes), `l01_37_oracle::every_step_unit_agrees` (130 native runs, 14 compared), the usage line in `l01_21_test_runner::command`, and the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding`. `tools/run_tests.sh 4`: 98 pieces, 1 failed, `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::every_printed_type_parses_back_to_itself` (25 `#ioError(_)` types of L1 units, failing on main since 4d82672, item L29-print).
- OSP-lit: read failures now carry `#ioError(op, path, why)`, replacing the bare `#ioError` of the L1.19 note above; the types still print the atom as `#ioError(_)` at L1. The path of a `Lines` port's error is the port's name. A bare pattern `#a` in `else` matches `#a` with any payload or none (DESIGN L1.27 allows it). A doc link resolves against every binder of the unit and the prelude, not by scope. An unattached doc block is an error, since the checker has no warnings. A float literal is limited by its rounded value only; the cost of reading the digits of a long int or rat literal is that of `num-bigint`'s decimal parse, which is quadratic in the digits (not changed here).
- OSP-drop: the statement-position parts (a statement `e else d;` and `*.`) are left to L6.3. The match arm `_ ->` is a pattern, not a binding (DESIGN.md's table of `_`), and `match` arrives at L2.4, so the test checks only that it is refused as L2.4 and not as a drop. `(_, x) = …` is not a binding at L1 (`TupleParam` is L2), so nested `_` is tested as the target list `_, x = …`. The repair note writes `_ = … else #ok;` and does not quote the value, since the checker has no source text and `Expr`'s display is prefix form. A default that is not an atom literal (a name, a call) names no atoms: its own loose atoms are walked like any other value's.
- OSP-drop2: a name bound by `name = e` now carries the naming of `e`: `y = lookup(1) else #notFound; _ = y;` is accepted, where OSP-drop rejected it. The set kept per expression may hold more atoms than its type, where a part's failure stops there (`==` gives a `bool`); `_` keeps only those of the type it binds, so the extra atoms can only reject. `Ty::dropped` is no longer used by the checker, only by `discard.rs`. The match criterion is written as an ignored test, since resolve refuses `match` until L2.4. `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::every_printed_type_parses_back_to_itself` fails on this branch and on its base b9115b2 alike (25 printed types such as `#ioError(_)` hold a form of layer L2); it is not caused here.
- L1.19a: DESIGN did not say how a `main` test row gives a `CommandLine` port; it is a `String` with one argument per line, split as a `Lines` input is, so an argument holding a newline cannot be given by a row (the spec sentence is on `wt/l1-19a-spec`). Words of other `zedc` commands that are not UTF-8 are usage errors too, since `main.rs` now reads `args_os`. Not fixed here: DESIGN says a record names its program's content address, and `Record` holds no address, so a replay of a record against a changed program is not caught; this predates L1.19a.
- CHK-cost (with CHK-curried and CHK-carrier; COMPLEXITY F77): nested tuples n^3 -> linear (39k instructions per node), nested lambdas n^2 -> linear (62k per node), `+` 1.1M -> 140k instructions per node, negation 390k -> 65k, call 340k -> 80k. Type variables keep their class name until `finish` writes the names once, so curried lambdas using every parameter are linear (8.4 times the instructions, 8.2 times the bytes at n = 100 and 800). A class solved to a carrier is replaced outside the lambdas only and read through the union-find inside them (`Ck::stale`): curried lambdas whose parameters take a carrier 8.0 times the instructions and 7.9 times the bytes (n^2 before), test no longer ignored; a chain of calls `g(0)(1)…(n)` is linear by a memoized `Sig::digest`. Repair 5: `is` tests compare such lambdas through the resolver (`Ty::diff_with`); before, the branch where a tuple holding one is not of the tested type kept it, in programs already rejected because a tuple takes no function. `curried_classes` checks 25 programs against the output of the build that replaces classes at every depth. Open: a held function at the bottom of n nested tuples is n^2 in its error notes (ledger CHK-held-cascade).

## Open points

- After L1.37a, the feature build (`--features short-addresses`) fails tests that check the hash itself, by design, and these run in the full build only: `l01_32` `facet_order` (equal and different addresses), `stability::the_address_is_pinned`, `fuzz::the_key_agrees_with_the_address`; `m0_07` `rules::the_version_tag_separates_rule_sets`, `alpha::addresses_agree_with_an_independent_alpha_equivalence_test`; `m0_11` `units::the_address_is_pinned`. Some scaling tests also exceed their limits in that build. Measured after repair #1, which keeps the lowering's nodes of one key in a map ordered by shape: `l01_33` 43 of 43 and `l01_37a_short_addresses` 16 of 16 pass there (the earlier chain failed 3 and 2 of them, on bytes allocated, 24.1 to 34.0 times against 24); still failing are `l01_20` `scaling::the_term_of_every_fact_scales`, `m0_09` `provenance::the_check_for_repeated_addresses_on_a_chain_of_products_scales`, `l01_26` `literals::adopting_the_literals_of_a_deep_tuple_scales` and `l01_23` `listing::taking_k_values_costs_k_times_the_depth`, and the rest of `l01_23` passes the 60 s limit there. None of these lower ZIR. Profiled (perf, 8-bit build) for the `l01_26` and `l01_23` tests: most cycles go to comparing type contents where the 8-bit digests tie (`Ord` of `Type`, `Tuples`, `Lits`, `Atoms`, of ordered sets of `Value`, and `bdd::order`), so each comparison costs the size of the types instead of O(1). The `l01_20` and `m0_09` failures and the deep-stack tests of `l01_16`, `l01_22`, `l01_31`, `l01_34` (which timed out before repair #1, not rerun since) are not profiled; their cause is inferred to be the same. The local suite runs `l01_37a_short_addresses` and `l01_37_oracle` in that build (tools/run_tests.sh, `short-*` pieces, target `build/short`).
- Open after L1.37a: the BDD atom order still follows the type digests, so the printed text of a union of tuple types less another follows them too. Measured: `(| (tuple 0 #a0) (tuple 1 #a1)) - (tuple 1 _)` prints `(| (& (tuple 0 #a0) (! (tuple 1 _))) (& (tuple 1 #a1) (! (tuple 1 _))))` in the full build and `(| (& (tuple 0 #a0) (! (tuple 1 _))) (& (tuple 1 #a1) (! (tuple 0 #a0)) (! (tuple 1 _))))` in the 8-bit build. Both are the same type; no L1 source prints one, and the case is left out of `record::printed_types` with a comment. Fix: order the BDD atoms by a structural key, or print from a normal form that does not depend on the order; ledger item BDD-atom-order (needs L1.37a, feeds L1.40).
- After L1.37a, an import's `pin` is compared by address alone: there are no contents to fall back on, since the pinned unit may be absent. Not checked at L1. The weak topological order (`wto`) breaks ties by address and then by form, so its order differs between the two builds; its results do not (`m0_05` passes in both).
- After L1.17a: `Ord` on numbers cannot fail, so after a refused comparison it gives the order of the normal forms until the run stops with the refusal. A sort inside one run that meets both a refused pair and the same pair by value could see an inconsistent order; the run still stops with the refusal, but the sort's result before that is not specified.
- The instruction count of `zed_tests::scaling` does not show the order of large copies done
  by vector moves (T11-fix-rest, F46): the old `resolve` took 10.2 times the instructions at
  8 times the size, but 51.8 times the processor time. A scaling test of code whose cost is
  copying memory needs a second measure, such as bytes allocated or copied (ledger item
  SCALE-bytes). Done: SCALE-bytes (3fa1fdc) checks bytes allocated next to instructions.
- After T14-occ (COMPLEXITY.md K25): `Provenance::origins` reads a family entry by entry when
  no family it was made from, or made from it, has been read, at the cost of the set of
  failures each entry holds, so `n` entries holding one set of `m` failures cost `n·m` (no
  worse than before). Since ADDR-keys (COMPLEXITY.md K40), `deep.rs` and `origins` key shared
  sequences by `Items::id`, not by address.
- From OSP-diag: (a) Fixed by OSP-neglit: `-1.abs`, the case of the rejection row "Prefix `-` on a
  numeric literal's postfix chain", is rejected by the AST builder. (b) Fixed in repair #1: the rows "Named argument before a positional one"
  and "`positional` parameter after a named one" are set by the AST builder. (c) The
  `for T in …` clause DESIGN decides for printed bounds is not in the `Type` grammar. (d) The
  printed-type check leaves out what type syntax cannot state: families, payloads, the names
  of positional parameters, defaults, needs, ownership and data. (e) Features `printed-types`
  and `counts` are on in any build that includes `zed-tests`, since cargo unifies features in
  a workspace build; a release build of `zedc` alone has both off. A `default-members` list
  without `tests` would fix a plain `cargo build` but not `cargo build --workspace`, which
  builds every member; a release is built per binary (`cargo build --release -p zedc`).

- Corpus outputs that rest on rules the spec has not fixed yet; each is a choice stated in
  the program's `ref/NN.py`, to be revisited at the step that fixes the rule: the `.window`
  signature and late-drop rule (programs 17, 18, 29; step L7.5), where a parse error is
  reported (7, 30), `serialize` writing no whitespace, `++` on int-keyed sets reading in key
  order, `zip` keeping the overlap of two rank ranges (25), stdin keyed by rank from 0.
  The programs are only parsed and layer-checked so far, not type-checked or run.
- Programs 14 and 15 are labeled L7 for incremental reuse (L7.6), though their syntax only
  reaches L6.

## Postmortem amendments (spec commit after 678e2d1)

The postmortem of related languages (array, dataflow, Datalog and incremental languages; report kept outside the repo) proposed nine amendments. Applied to DESIGN.md:
- A3: `@export("c", "symbol")` lets C call pure Zed functions (sec:ffi; tested in L7.4).
- A1: `zed build --explain-plan` (L5.10).
- A2: a lowering stays within a constant factor of `f::cost`; `@cost(depth: …)`.
- A6: recompute or incremental is chosen per wave by cost; the L7 gate adds a 50%-dirty check.
- A7: the L5 ratios are one core against one core, and efficiency is measured against the Rust baseline. L5.14 now agrees with the gate table.

Not applied:
- A4 (name a first domain): a product decision with no effect on what is built.
- A5 (license): the repo already has LICENSE; the choice belongs to the owner.
- A8 (two-process test at L7): distribution is post-v1 in sec:targets.
- A9 (count programs needing generations): no decision depends on the count.

## M0.3 — desugaring rules (done, in the spec)

The spec's sec:grammar now has a `desugar` fence with one rule per syntactic production (107), and the spec repo's `tools/grammar/check.py` checks that rules and productions match one to one. A negative control (an extra rule and a duplicate) is caught. Nothing in this repo changes until the L1 elaborator, which implements these rules. Decisions:
- `&&` and `||` desugar to `if`, not to the strict primitives; the spec calls them selection nodes that short-circuit observationally.
- Recursion enters at L6. A recursive function is the definedness fix over its parameter domain, evaluated on demand. The M0.9 evaluator computes that fix over a finite domain only; the L1/L6 interpreter evaluates on demand.
- Kernel primitives the rules name that the M0.9 kernel lacks: `pow` (L1), `range` (L3), and the type test `is` (L1). They are added at those steps.
- `break e`: the step's guard returns `#break(e)` and the loop's result is `e`.
- T14 and T11 findings are now nodes in `docs/plan_dag.md`, with a table placing each finding
  (section "T14 and T11 findings"): T14-bugs-rt, T14-bugs-kernel, T14-value, T14-heap,
  T14-hooks, T14-rope, T14-bring, T14-paths, T14-provenance, T14-low, T14-rest, T11-preimage,
  T11-ty, T11-check, T11-elab. T14 itself blocks nothing; T12 needs every fix node, and
  L1.41 needs T12 (since 4420380, L1.40 needs only T4, ST1 and ST2). Plan only: no code changed.
- `Prim::TupleGet` on a `fix` under construction is a type error, `TupleGet applied to a function`: the Partial case in `Machine::prim` tests only `Prim::Get`, while `elab.rs` gives `v[0]` as `TupleGet`. No term reaches it today, since `elab.rs` makes no `Term::Fix`; the elaboration of definedness `fix` must write `Get` or the case must take both, as the port case does.
